# Part 0: PostgreSQL and the Product Rename Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Move the hub from MySQL 8.4 to PostgreSQL 18 from a fresh, empty database, re-derive every MySQL-specific behaviour the code relied on, and rename the product to "AI App Hub", with no other change in behaviour.

**Architecture:** The hub talks to its own `postgres:18` container through `pg` (node-postgres) and `drizzle-orm/node-postgres`, with one `Pool` per process and an `error` listener on it. The schema moves from mysql-core to pg-core (`uuid` ids generated by the app, `timestamptz`, `text`, `jsonb`, `bytea`, enums for the static sets), and one generated baseline migration replaces the MySQL history; the container's entrypoint still applies it. What MySQL did implicitly becomes explicit: a `lower(email)` unique index with `lower()` on both sides of every lookup, a partial unique index for the single owner, SQLSTATE codes, `rowCount`, `ON CONFLICT`, indexes on referencing columns, and a `z.uuid()` check on every id from outside.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16 (App Router, Server Actions, Route Handlers), Drizzle ORM and drizzle-kit 1.0.0-rc.4, `pg` 8.23.1 and `@types/pg` 8.23.1 (new), PostgreSQL 18.6 (`postgres:18` image), zod 4.6.5, Vitest 4 (node environment; projects `unit`, `ldap`, and the new `db`), Playwright 1.63, Docker Compose v2.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-part-0-postgres-and-rename-design.md` (owner-approved 2026-10-11, decision 27). Programme: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-provider-hub-charter.md`. Docs check: `docs/superpowers/research/2026-10-11-provider-hub/doc-verification.md` (rows cited as "DV 1a" and so on). Running record: `docs/superpowers/research/2026-10-11-provider-hub/README.md` (decisions 13–16, 23–28).

## Global Constraints

- Branch `feat/postgres-and-rename` from `fork/overhaul` (`477d261d`), created when execution starts. PR to `fork/overhaul` only when the owner says so.
- Commits: conventional (`feat|fix|refactor|test|docs|chore(scope): …`), English, each ending with the session's trailers `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` and `Claude-Session: <the session's link>` in one `-m` argument (separate `-m` flags leave only the last line parsed as a trailer). `git add <paths>`, never `-A`. Nothing is pushed until the owner says so.
- ADR-0002: documented approaches only; every non-obvious choice carries its source in a comment, as the surrounding code does. Sources for this plan: the DV rows, the docs named in each task, and the measurements in "Plan decisions" (made against `postgres:18` 18.6 with drizzle-orm 1.0.0-rc.4 and `pg` 8.23.1).
- Gates before each commit (CLAUDE.md): `pnpm exec next typegen && pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec oxlint <files>`, `pnpm exec oxfmt --check <files>`, `pnpm test`. From Task 4 on, a task that touches the data layer also runs `pnpm test:db`.
- AGENTS.md: migrations through `pnpm db:generate` and `pnpm db:migrate` only, never `drizzle-kit push`; the generated SQL is read before it is committed. `.cii-assessment.md` is re-checked before the last commit and committed on its own if a row changes (Task 13).
- Database image `postgres:18` (18.6, supported to 2030-11-14). Credentials `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (required, non-empty), `POSTGRES_USER`, `POSTGRES_DB` (DV 7a).
- Volume `pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql`, never `/var/lib/postgresql/data` (the 18 images moved `VOLUME`; DV 7b). The e2e service uses `tmpfs` at `/var/lib/postgresql`.
- `shm_size: 128mb` on every `postgres` service (DV 7c).
- Healthcheck `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}` with `interval: 10s`, `timeout: 10s`, `retries: 5`, `start_period: 30s`; the app waits on `condition: service_healthy` (DV 7d; Compose "Control startup order").
- Ports: local PostgreSQL `127.0.0.1:5432` only; e2e PostgreSQL `127.0.0.1:5433` with fixed credentials `e2e`/`e2e`/`e2e`.
- Driver `pg` through `drizzle-orm/node-postgres`: `new Pool({ connectionString, connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000 })`, `pool.on('error', …)` logging name and code only, `drizzle({ client: pool, logger })`; the `globalThis` key `aiAppHubDb` (DV 1a, 1b; Plan decision f).
- `drizzle-orm` and `drizzle-kit` exactly `1.0.0-rc.4`; `mysql2` removed (Task 3).
- Ids: PostgreSQL `uuid`, generated by the app (`crypto.randomUUID()` in the DAL, version 4), never by the database. Every id from outside passes `z.uuid()` before a query; a missed one raises `22P02`, which answers as "not found" (spec §3.2).
- Timestamps: `timestamp with time zone`, no precision (`timestamptz()` helper, DV 2a, 2b). Strings: `text`, never `varchar(n)`; limits live in zod (DV 11, ADR-0025). Images: `bytea`. JSON: `jsonb`. Static sets: `pgEnum`.
- SQLSTATE codes: `23505` unique_violation, `23503` foreign_key_violation, `40P01` deadlock_detected, `22P02` invalid_text_representation, read from the driver error under Drizzle's `DrizzleQueryError.cause`; the `constraint` field names the index where a table has several. Logs carry the error's name and code only, never `detail`, the message or the parameters (DV 5a, 5b).
- Names: `ai-app-hub` (package, image, compose projects, folders) and "AI App Hub" (the built-in brand). The built-in logo `assets/images/logo.png` stays as it is (owner, 2026-10-11). Table names stay (`dify_apps` until Part 1).
- Unit tests run in Vitest's node environment (no DOM). Browser behaviour is proven by the e2e suite on three projects (`desktop-light`, `desktop-dark`, `mobile-light`); stop `pnpm dev` before `pnpm test:e2e`.
- Never read or print `.env` or `.env*.local`. The owner edits them (Task 14 stops and asks). Never send an email address to any service.
- Scratch files go to the repo's git-ignored `tmp/`, never `/tmp`; nothing under `tmp/` ends in `.ts` (tsc includes `**/*.ts`).
- The plan's own formatter (`oxfmt`) strips the leading tabs of a code fragment taken from inside a function. Find each "replace" fragment by its text, and keep the indentation of the lines it replaces. Line numbers are those of `477d261d` unless a step says otherwise; earlier tasks shift some of them, so the quoted text decides.

## Review Focus

1. **Signing in with the email typed in another case** (`Jane@Example.com` for the account `jane@example.com`, on the local tab; and a directory entry whose email is a case variant of an existing account). Expected: the local sign-in finds the account; the directory sign-in sees the email as taken (`email_in_use`, the generic message) exactly as MySQL's collation did. On PostgreSQL a plain `=` is case-sensitive, so without Task 6 the first returns "wrong credentials" and the second throws. Pinned in Task 6 (rendered SQL `lower("users"."email") = lower($1)` in all four lookups, and a live `pnpm test:db` case for each) and Task 11 (e2e, both tabs).
2. **The first start on a fresh volume** (the owner's first `up` after Part 0, and every new server). Expected: the hub starts only once PostgreSQL answers on TCP, applies the baseline once and does not restart. Without `-h 127.0.0.1`, `pg_isready` can answer "ready" from the init-time server that listens only on the socket, and the hub's migration fails once. Pinned in Task 3 (`__tests__/compose-files.test.ts` pins the healthcheck, the volume path and `service_healthy`; `docker compose config` validates the files) and Task 14 (the Docker gate checks one "Database migration complete" line and a restart count of 0).
3. **A PostgreSQL restart under a running hub** (`docker compose restart postgres`, a host reboot, an upgrade). Expected: the hub logs one line per idle client (`Database pool: { name, code: '57P01' }`) and answers the next request once the server is back; it does not crash. Without the pool's `error` listener Node throws the event and the process exits. Pinned in Task 2 (`__tests__/db-pool.test.ts`), Task 5 (the log carries name and code only) and Task 14 (the restart check).
4. **A malformed id in a URL** (`/chat/abc`, `/api/dify/abc/parameters`, `/api/apps/abc/icon`, a truncated link, an action called with `'u9'`). Expected: the same answer as an unknown id (the "missing" page, 404 `app_not_found`, `not_found`), never a 500. PostgreSQL's `uuid` type raises `22P02` on such a string. Pinned in Task 9 (the guard, the page, the icon route and the action schemas refuse it before any query), Task 5 (`22P02` maps to not found as a safety net) and Task 11 (e2e).
5. **Two accounts whose emails differ only in case** (an admin creates `Jane@example.com` while `jane@example.com` exists, or two such writes race). Expected: refused as "email in use"; the database itself refuses the second row. Pinned in Task 2 (the baseline's `users_email_key` on `lower("email")`), Task 4 (a live insert is refused with `23505 users_email_key`), Task 5 and Task 6 (`createUser` answers `email_in_use` live) and Task 11 (the users page).

Also pinned, less likely to bite: two first-run submissions at once leave exactly one owner (Task 7, live); a second sync container claiming the same slot skips (Task 8, live).

## Plan decisions (taken while planning, where the spec leaves the choice open)

Measured while planning: a scratch project with drizzle-orm and drizzle-kit 1.0.0-rc.4 and `pg` 8.23.1 against a throwaway `postgres:18` (18.6) container. It generated the baseline below from the planned schema, applied it, and checked the error fields, `rowCount`, `ON CONFLICT … RETURNING`, two concurrent first runs, the pool's `57P01` event and psql's variable over stdin. Where a step says "measured while planning", this is the source.

- **a. Drizzle 1.0.0-rc.4 lands first, on the MySQL code (Task 1).** A failure then belongs to the release, not to the port. The rc.4 release notes list nothing that changes this code's MySQL imports (`gh release view v1.0.0-rc.4 -R drizzle-team/drizzle-orm`).
- **b. One task switches the dialect (Task 2).** Drizzle types the database, the tables and every query by one dialect (`NodePgDatabase`, pg-core tables), so tsc and the unit tests can only be green when the driver, the schema, the compile-level fixes and the tests' driver mocks move together. The behaviour re-derivations follow, each with its own red test: errors (Task 5), email (6), owner (7), conflicts (8), ids (9). Between Tasks 2 and 5 the code runs on PostgreSQL with MySQL's error codes, so Task 3 runs only the two e2e specs that need neither. The full suite runs in Tasks 11 and 14.
- **c. A third Vitest project, `db` (`pnpm test:db`, Task 4).** It runs against the e2e compose file's PostgreSQL, on a database of its own (`hub_dal`), recreated and migrated by its `globalSetup`. It copies the `ldap` project's pattern (decision j; Vitest "Test Projects", `globalSetup`). An index, a conflict path or a driver error field cannot be proven with a mock, and the e2e suite cannot call the Data Access Layer (`e2e/fixtures/users.ts` loads none of the app's modules). Tasks 5–8 add their live cases; the project is a gate for those tasks and for Task 14.
- **d. The enum types are named for their sets and exported with the tables.**
  - The names are `user_role`, `account_source`, `access_mode`, `membership_source`, `sync_trigger` and `sync_outcome`. Two columns are called `source` and hold different sets, so a type cannot be named after its column.
  - drizzle-kit reads enum types only from the schema module's exports (drizzle-kit 1.0.0-rc.4 `fromExports`; Drizzle's enum example exports `moodEnum`), so `db/schema/index.ts` exports them.
- **e. Group names stay unique without case: `user_groups_name_key` is on `lower(name)`.**
  - MySQL's collation made group names case-insensitive, and the code relies on it: B3a deviation 2, pinned by `e2e/admin-groups.spec.ts` ("The unique index compares names without case") and `__tests__/group-management-actions.test.ts`.
  - The spec names only the email (§3.4), but §1 promises no other change in behaviour.
  - The index is the same documented form as the email's (PostgreSQL 18 "Indexes on Expressions"). Accents now count for group names too, and ADR-0031 records it with the email change.
- **f. The pool gives up on a new connection after 10 s (`connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000`).** node-postgres has no limit by default ("Pool": "By default this is 0 which means no timeout"), while mysql2's `connectTimeout` defaulted to 10,000 ms. Without the option, a database that does not answer would hold the login page indefinitely instead of 10 s (ADR-0030's recorded limit; spec §1). ADR-0030 gets a dated note (Task 13).
- **g. `timestamptz(name)`, one column helper (`db/schema/columns.ts`).** It writes `timestamp(name, { withTimezone: true, mode: 'date' })` once, without a precision (DV 2a, 2b), as LobeHub's Drizzle schema does (`packages/database/src/schemas/_helpers.ts`, cited in DV 2b).
- **h. The error predicates take PostgreSQL's condition names.**
  - They are `isUniqueViolation(error, constraint?)`, `isForeignKeyViolation`, `isDeadlock` and `isInvalidTextRepresentation` (PostgreSQL 18 Appendix A: `unique_violation`, `foreign_key_violation`, `deadlock_detected`, `invalid_text_representation`). They read `code` and `constraint` from the error or its `cause`, as `hasCode` reads today.
  - A caller names the constraint wherever a table has more than one unique index (DV 5b): `users_email_key` for `email_in_use`, `user_groups_name_key` for `name_in_use`.
- **i. The `22P02` safety net sits where an unexpected error becomes an answer.** `toActionFailure` answers `not_found` and `errorResponseFrom` answers 404 `app_not_found`; both log the error by name and code. A missed id check therefore shows in the log and never as a 500 (spec §3.2).
- **j. One id check, `lib/ids.ts` (`idSchema = z.uuid()`, `isId`).** `userIdSchema` and `groupIdSchema` become aliases of it. ADR-0024 decision h ("account ids are not checked as UUIDs", because older rows might use another format) ends with the fresh start: every id is the hub's own UUID (decision 14).
- **k. `emailMatches(email)` (`lib/data/email.ts`) applies `lower()` to both sides in SQL.**
  - `lower()` itself is exported from `db/schema/users.ts`, where Drizzle's guide "Unique and Case-Insensitive Email Handling" puts it.
  - That guide lowers the value in JavaScript instead; the plan follows PostgreSQL's citext page (`lower(col) = LOWER(?)`), as DV change 1 chose, so the value goes through the same function as the index.
- **l. The e2e fixtures match the way MySQL did.** Their `LIKE` clean-ups become `ILIKE`, and `seedUser` deletes a leftover by `lower(email)`, because MySQL's collation compared without case and the clean-ups relied on it (PostgreSQL 18 "LIKE": "The key word ILIKE can be used instead of LIKE to make the match case-insensitive").
- **m. `cutToColumn` keeps its 255-code-point bound as the hub's own limit (Task 10).** The columns are `text` now (DV 11), and zod keeps the form limits (ADR-0025).
- **n. The production template gets `name: ai-app-hub` (Task 3).** That makes its volume `ai-app-hub_pgdata` whatever the folder is called (Compose file reference, top-level `name`). The image is `${DOCKERHUB_USERNAME:?…}/ai-app-hub:latest` (Compose "Interpolation", `${VAR:?error}`).
- **o. Renames the spec's §4 table leaves out.** `.github/workflows/deploy-docs.yml` filters on the docs package's name and is renamed with it (Task 12). `AGENTS.md` (upstream's) keeps `pnpm --filter dify-app-hub db:*`, which no longer matches; `pnpm db:generate` and `pnpm db:migrate` from the root are the working form, and `CLAUDE.md` says so (Task 13).
- **p. The owner-password recovery in `docs/auth-gate.md` runs psql with a variable over stdin.**
  - Form: `-v hash="$HASH"` on the command line, `:'hash'` in the SQL.
  - psql's `-c` must be "completely parsable by the server (i.e., it contains no psql-specific features)", so a variable needs stdin. Measured on `postgres:18`: a `$2b$…` hash reaches the row intact.
- **q. `createOwner` keeps its locking read; its comment changes (Task 7).** `lockAnyAccount` still finds a row when the table is not empty. Its comment now says that PostgreSQL locks only rows a read returns, so the partial index is the guarantee. The directory sign-in keeps its explicit `read committed` (PostgreSQL's default; DV 7 says it can stay).
- **r. The old local stack is taken down by its project name (Task 14).** The command is `docker compose -p dify-app-hub-local down`, after a `--dry-run` (Compose CLI `-p, --project-name`). The branch's compose file names the new project. Measured: Compose v5.1.1 finds the old project by name without its file.
- **s. The owner's rename steps stay in spec §8, with `CLAUDE.md` pointing there (Task 13).** They run once, between sessions; a second copy would drift.
- **t. Task 2's test port adds the `rowCount: null` cases spec §6 asks for** ("Every `rowCount` consumer handles `null`"): the directory writes and `deleteGroup`.

### Task 1: Drizzle 1.0.0-rc.4 on the current MySQL code

The version bump runs on its own, before the switch, so a failure here is the release's and not the port's (Plan decision a).

**Files:**

- Modify: `package.json` (line 38 `"drizzle-orm": "1.0.0-rc.3"`, line 68 `"drizzle-kit": "1.0.0-rc.3"`), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (by pnpm)

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: `drizzle-orm` and `drizzle-kit` at exactly `1.0.0-rc.4`; every later task builds on them.

- [ ] **Step 1: Read the release notes**

Run: `gh release view v1.0.0-rc.4 -R drizzle-team/drizzle-orm` Expected: the notes of 2026-06-27. The items that touch this code (read on 2026-10-11 while planning):

- "Improved typed `sql` operator - preserved nullability, inferred initial data type to `mapWith` callback argument, added `.nullable()`": the one `mapWith` in the code, `sql<boolean>\`${difyApps.iconImage} IS NOT NULL\`.mapWith(Boolean)` (`lib/data/apps.ts:48`), is not nullable, so its output type stays `boolean`.
- "Split `mysql` into `async`, `effect` versions" and "Added codec system to `mysql`": the MySQL code still imports `drizzle-orm/mysql2` and `drizzle-orm/mysql-core`, which rc.4 still ships (`drizzle.mock`, `QueryBuilder`, `getTableConfig`, `mysqlEnum`, `datetime`, `mediumblob`); Task 2 removes every MySQL import.
- "Fixed `mysql` cropping `.000` from `timestamp(3)`": MySQL only; gone after Task 2.
- Drizzle Kit "Auto-detected non-interactive mode (`--output json`, or `--output text` with a non-TTY stdin) no longer renders interactive prompts. Instead, unresolved renames and destructive changes … are surfaced as a `missing_hints` envelope": Task 2 generates the baseline into an empty folder, where there is nothing to rename or confirm.
- Nothing else names an API this code uses (the RQBv1, SQLite, MSSQL, Effect and driver items).

- [ ] **Step 2: Bump both packages, exact versions as today**

Run: `pnpm add --save-exact drizzle-orm@1.0.0-rc.4 && pnpm add --save-exact -D drizzle-kit@1.0.0-rc.4` Expected: `package.json` reads `"drizzle-orm": "1.0.0-rc.4"` and `"drizzle-kit": "1.0.0-rc.4"`; `pnpm-lock.yaml` changes.

- [ ] **Step 3: Run the gates on the unchanged code**

Run: `pnpm exec next typegen && pnpm exec tsc --noEmit && pnpm test` Expected: tsc reports nothing; every unit test passes (the count before the bump; nothing new). A failure here is the release's: record it and stop before Task 2.

- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**

```bash
git add package.json pnpm-lock.yaml
git commit -m "chore(deps): update drizzle-orm and drizzle-kit to 1.0.0-rc.4"
```

### Task 2: The switch to PostgreSQL: driver, connection, schema and baseline

Drizzle types the database, the tables and every query by one dialect, so the driver, the schema, the compile-level fixes in the data layer and the unit tests' driver mocks move together in one commit (Plan decision b). Behaviour stays as it is: the error codes, the email lookups, the owner's isolation level, the conflict paths and the id checks keep their MySQL-era form until Tasks 5–9 re-derive them, each with its own tests.

**Files:**

- Create: `db/schema/columns.ts`, `db/schema/enums.ts`, `db/migrations/<timestamp>_baseline/{migration.sql,snapshot.json}` (generated)

- Produces: `getDb(): Db` with `type Db = ReturnType<typeof createDb>` (a `NodePgDatabase` with `$client: Pool`, `connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000`), the `globalThis` key `aiAppHubDb`; `lower(column: AnyPgColumn): SQL` from `db/schema/users.ts`; `timestamptz(name: string)` from `db/schema/columns.ts`; the enums `userRole` (`user_role`), `accountSource` (`account_source`), `accessMode` (`access_mode`), `membershipSource` (`membership_source`), `syncTrigger` (`sync_trigger`), `syncOutcome` (`sync_outcome`), exported from `@/db/schema` with every table; the indexes `users_email_key` (on `lower("email")`), `users_directory_id_key`, `users_one_owner` (partial), `user_groups_name_key` (on `lower("name")`), `directory_sync_runs_slot_key`, `app_group_grants_group_id_idx`, `app_user_grants_user_id_idx`, `user_group_members_user_id_idx`; `parseTags(value: unknown): string[]`; one migration folder `db/migrations/<14 digits>_baseline`. Write results are pg's `QueryResult` (`rowCount: number | null`), read as `(result.rowCount ?? 0)`.

Create the baseline test:

```ts
// __tests__/db-baseline.test.ts
import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs'
import path from 'node:path'

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

const folders = readdirSync('db/migrations')
const sql = () => readFileSync(path.join('db/migrations', folders[0]!, 'migration.sql'), 'utf8')

describe('the PostgreSQL baseline (Part 0 spec §3.3)', () => {
	it('is the only migration: the MySQL history is gone (a fresh start, decision 14)', () => {
		expect(folders).toHaveLength(1)
		expect(folders[0]).toMatch(/^\d{14}_baseline$/)
	})

	it('creates the six enum types for the static sets', () => {
		for (const type of [
			`CREATE TYPE "access_mode" AS ENUM('everyone', 'restricted');`,
			`CREATE TYPE "account_source" AS ENUM('local', 'ldap');`,
			`CREATE TYPE "membership_source" AS ENUM('manual', 'directory');`,
			`CREATE TYPE "sync_outcome" AS ENUM('running', 'succeeded', 'failed', 'empty', 'id_attribute_changed');`,
			`CREATE TYPE "sync_trigger" AS ENUM('schedule', 'startup', 'manual');`,
			`CREATE TYPE "user_role" AS ENUM('owner', 'admin', 'user');`,
		])
			expect(sql()).toContain(type)
	})

	it('creates the eleven tables, with their names kept', () => {
		for (const table of [
			'app_group_grants',
			'app_user_grants',
			'dify_apps',
			'directory_sync_runs',
			'instance_setting_files',
			'instance_settings',
			'password_reset_tokens',
			'user_group_directory_links',
			'user_group_members',
			'user_groups',
			'users',
		])
			expect(sql()).toContain(`CREATE TABLE "${table}" (`)
		expect(sql().match(/CREATE TABLE/g)).toHaveLength(11)
	})

	// PostgreSQL 18 "Indexes on Expressions": a unique index on lower(col1) "would prevent creation of rows whose col1
	// values differ only in case" (spec §3.4).
	it('keeps emails unique whatever their case', () => {
		expect(sql()).toContain('CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "users_email_key" ON "users" (lower("email"));')
	})

	// MySQL's collation compared group names without case too (Plan decision e).
	it('keeps group names unique whatever their case', () => {
		expect(sql()).toContain(
			'CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "user_groups_name_key" ON "user_groups" (lower("name"));',
		)
	})

	// PostgreSQL 18 "Partial Indexes", Example 11.3 (spec §3.4).
	it('allows one owner through a partial unique index', () => {
		expect(sql()).toContain(
			`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "users_one_owner" ON "users" ("role") WHERE "role" = 'owner';`,
		)
	})

	// PostgreSQL 18 "Foreign Keys": "the declaration of a foreign key constraint does not automatically create an index
	// on the referencing columns"; InnoDB created one (spec §3.2).
	it('indexes the referencing columns that no primary key leads with', () => {
		for (const index of [
			'CREATE INDEX "app_group_grants_group_id_idx" ON "app_group_grants" ("group_id");',
			'CREATE INDEX "app_user_grants_user_id_idx" ON "app_user_grants" ("user_id");',
			'CREATE INDEX "user_group_members_user_id_idx" ON "user_group_members" ("user_id");',
		])
			expect(sql()).toContain(index)
	})

	it('cascades all eight foreign keys on delete', () => {
		expect(sql().match(/FOREIGN KEY/g)).toHaveLength(8)
		expect(
			sql().match(/FOREIGN KEY \("[a-z_]+"\) REFERENCES "[a-z_]+"\("[a-z_]+"\) ON DELETE CASCADE/g),
		).toHaveLength(8)
	})

	// DV 2a, 2b, 2c, 11: timestamptz without a precision, text, bytea; nothing MySQL-shaped is left.
	it('uses PostgreSQL types only', () => {
		expect(sql()).toContain('"created_at" timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL')
		expect(sql()).not.toMatch(/timestamp\(|varchar|datetime|mediumblob|`/)
	})
})
```

Create the pool test:

```ts
// __tests__/db-pool.test.ts
import { DatabaseError } from 'pg'
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'

vi.mock('@/lib/env', () => ({
	env: () => ({ databaseUrl: 'postgres://hub:hub@127.0.0.1:1/hub', nodeEnv: 'test' }),
}))

import { getDb } from '@/db'

afterEach(() => {
	vi.restoreAllMocks()
})

describe('the database pool (spec §3.1, DV 1b)', () => {
	it('is one pool per process', () => {
		expect(getDb()).toBe(getDb())
	})

	// Spec §1: nothing else changes. node-postgres has no connect timeout by default; mysql2 gave up after 10 s.
	it('gives up on a new connection after 10 s, as the MySQL driver did', () => {
		expect(getDb().$client.options.connectionTimeoutMillis).toBe(10_000)
	})

	// node-postgres "Pool", events: an idle client's error reaches the pool's `error` event, and "if a pool emits an
	// error event and no listeners are added node will emit an uncaught error and potentially crash your node process".
	// A PostgreSQL restart ends every idle connection with 57P01 (measured against postgres:18 while planning).
	it('keeps an idle client’s error from the process and logs it with its context', () => {
		const logged = vi.spyOn(console, 'error').mockImplementation(() => {})
		const terminated = new DatabaseError(
			'terminating connection due to administrator command',
			0,
			'error',
		)
		terminated.code = '57P01'
		const pool = getDb().$client
		expect(pool.listenerCount('error')).toBe(1)
		expect(() => pool.emit('error', terminated)).not.toThrow()
		expect(logged).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)
		expect(logged.mock.calls[0]?.[0]).toBe('Database pool:')
	})
})
```

Port the four schema tests. Replace the whole of `__tests__/b3a-schema.test.ts` with (the "B3a migrations" block goes: its migrations are deleted, and `__tests__/db-baseline.test.ts` pins the baseline):

```ts
// __tests__/b3a-schema.test.ts
import { getTableConfig } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

import {
	appGroupGrants,
	appUserGrants,
	difyApps,
	userGroupMembers,
	userGroups,
	users,
} from '@/db/schema'

describe('the B3a schema (B3 spec §3.1)', () => {
	it('adds two deactivation markers and who deactivated, all nullable, to users', () => {
		expect(users.adminDeactivatedAt.notNull).toBe(false)
		expect(users.adminDeactivatedBy.notNull).toBe(false)
		expect(users.directoryDeactivatedAt.notNull).toBe(false)
		// No foreign key on who deactivated: deleting that admin keeps the record.
		expect(getTableConfig(users).foreignKeys).toEqual([])
	})

	it('closes a new app by default (spec §2 #9)', () => {
		expect(difyApps.accessMode.enumValues).toEqual(['everyone', 'restricted'])
		expect(difyApps.accessMode.getSQLType()).toBe('access_mode')
		expect(difyApps.accessMode.notNull).toBe(true)
		expect(difyApps.accessMode.default).toBe('restricted')
	})

	it('names groups uniquely and records the source of each membership', () => {
		expect(getTableConfig(userGroups).name).toBe('user_groups')
		expect(getTableConfig(userGroups).indexes.map(index => index.config.name)).toEqual([
			'user_groups_name_key',
		])
		expect(userGroupMembers.source.enumValues).toEqual(['manual', 'directory'])
		expect(userGroupMembers.source.getSQLType()).toBe('membership_source')
		const [primary] = getTableConfig(userGroupMembers).primaryKeys
		expect(primary.columns.map(column => column.name)).toEqual(['group_id', 'user_id', 'source'])
	})

	it.each([
		['user_group_members', userGroupMembers, ['user_groups', 'users']],
		['app_group_grants', appGroupGrants, ['dify_apps', 'user_groups']],
		['app_user_grants', appUserGrants, ['dify_apps', 'users']],
	] as const)('%s cascades every foreign key on delete', (_name, table, parents) => {
		const keys = getTableConfig(table).foreignKeys
		expect(keys.map(key => key.onDelete)).toEqual(parents.map(() => 'cascade'))
		expect(keys.map(key => getTableConfig(key.reference().foreignTable).name).sort()).toEqual(
			[...parents].sort(),
		)
	})

	// PostgreSQL 18 "Foreign Keys": no index on the referencing columns is created for a foreign key; InnoDB made one.
	// A composite primary key serves its leading column only, so the second referencing column gets its own (spec §3.2).
	it.each([
		['app_group_grants', appGroupGrants, 'app_group_grants_group_id_idx', 'group_id'],
		['app_user_grants', appUserGrants, 'app_user_grants_user_id_idx', 'user_id'],
		['user_group_members', userGroupMembers, 'user_group_members_user_id_idx', 'user_id'],
	] as const)('%s indexes its second referencing column', (_name, table, name, column) => {
		const [index] = getTableConfig(table).indexes
		expect(index.config.name).toBe(name)
		expect(index.config.unique).toBe(false)
		expect(index.config.columns.map(part => ('name' in part ? part.name : undefined))).toEqual([
			column,
		])
	})
})
```

```ts
// __tests__/b3b-schema.test.ts
import { getTableConfig } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

import { directorySyncRuns, userGroupDirectoryLinks, users } from '@/db/schema'

describe('the B3b schema (B3 spec §3.2)', () => {
	it('gives every account a source and lets a directory account have no password', () => {
		expect(users.source.enumValues).toEqual(['local', 'ldap'])
		expect(users.source.getSQLType()).toBe('account_source')
		expect(users.source.notNull).toBe(true)
		expect(users.source.default).toBe('local')
		expect(users.password.notNull).toBe(false)
		for (const column of [users.directoryId, users.directoryIdAttribute, users.directoryUsername])
			expect(column.notNull).toBe(false)
	})

	it('keeps one account per directory key, beside the unique email and the one owner', () => {
		const names = getTableConfig(users).indexes.map(index => index.config.name)
		expect(names).toEqual(['users_email_key', 'users_directory_id_key', 'users_one_owner'])
		const directoryKey = getTableConfig(users).indexes.find(
			index => index.config.name === 'users_directory_id_key',
		)!
		expect(directoryKey.config.unique).toBe(true)
	})

	it('ties the source to the credentials with a CHECK', () => {
		expect(getTableConfig(users).checks.map(check => check.name)).toEqual([
			'users_source_credentials',
		])
	})

	it('links a hub group to directory groups by key, cascading with the group', () => {
		const config = getTableConfig(userGroupDirectoryLinks)
		expect(config.name).toBe('user_group_directory_links')
		expect(config.primaryKeys[0].columns.map(column => column.name)).toEqual([
			'group_id',
			'directory_group_id',
		])
		expect(config.foreignKeys.map(key => key.onDelete)).toEqual(['cascade'])
		expect(getTableConfig(config.foreignKeys[0].reference().foreignTable).name).toBe('user_groups')
		expect(userGroupDirectoryLinks.missingSince.notNull).toBe(false)
	})

	it('records sync runs with a unique slot and the column name kept', () => {
		const config = getTableConfig(directorySyncRuns)
		expect(config.name).toBe('directory_sync_runs')
		expect(config.columns.map(column => column.name)).not.toContain('trigger')
		expect(directorySyncRuns.runTrigger.enumValues).toEqual(['schedule', 'startup', 'manual'])
		expect(directorySyncRuns.runTrigger.getSQLType()).toBe('sync_trigger')
		expect(directorySyncRuns.outcome.enumValues).toEqual([
			'running',
			'succeeded',
			'failed',
			'empty',
			'id_attribute_changed',
		])
		expect(directorySyncRuns.outcome.getSQLType()).toBe('sync_outcome')
		expect(directorySyncRuns.outcome.default).toBe('running')
		const slot = config.indexes.find(index => index.config.name === 'directory_sync_runs_slot_key')!
		expect(slot.config.unique).toBe(true)
		expect(config.foreignKeys).toEqual([])
	})
})
```

```ts
// __tests__/settings-schema.test.ts
import { getTableConfig } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

import { instanceSettingFiles, instanceSettings } from '@/db/schema'

describe('the settings schema (settings spec §3.2)', () => {
	it('keys instance_settings by area, stores a jsonb document, and keeps who saved it without a foreign key', () => {
		const config = getTableConfig(instanceSettings)
		expect(config.name).toBe('instance_settings')
		expect(instanceSettings.area.primary).toBe(true)
		expect(instanceSettings.area.getSQLType()).toBe('text')
		expect(instanceSettings.data.getSQLType()).toBe('jsonb')
		expect(instanceSettings.data.notNull).toBe(true)
		expect(instanceSettings.updatedBy.getSQLType()).toBe('uuid')
		expect(instanceSettings.updatedBy.notNull).toBe(false)
		// As users.admin_deactivated_by (B3 spec §3.1): deleting the saving account keeps the record.
		expect(config.foreignKeys).toEqual([])
	})

	it('keys instance_setting_files by area and name and deletes them with their area', () => {
		const config = getTableConfig(instanceSettingFiles)
		expect(config.name).toBe('instance_setting_files')
		const [primary] = config.primaryKeys
		expect(primary.columns.map(column => column.name)).toEqual(['area', 'name'])
		const [foreignKey] = config.foreignKeys
		expect(foreignKey.onDelete).toBe('cascade')
		expect(foreignKey.reference().foreignTable).toBe(instanceSettings)
		expect(instanceSettingFiles.bytes.getSQLType()).toBe('bytea')
		expect(instanceSettingFiles.mime.notNull).toBe(true)
	})
})
```

```ts
// __tests__/users-schema.test.ts
import { getTableConfig } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

import { users } from '@/db/schema'

describe('users schema', () => {
	it('stores the role as an enum that defaults to user (charter §4.4, ADR-0024)', () => {
		expect(users.role.enumValues).toEqual(['owner', 'admin', 'user'])
		expect(users.role.getSQLType()).toBe('user_role')
		expect(users.role.notNull).toBe(true)
		expect(users.role.default).toBe('user')
	})

	it('stamps updated_at on every update through Drizzle', () => {
		expect(users.updatedAt.onUpdateFn).toBeTypeOf('function')
	})

	// Spec §3.2: PostgreSQL's uuid type, the value made by the app so an id is known before the insert.
	it('keys an account by a uuid the app generates', () => {
		expect(users.id.getSQLType()).toBe('uuid')
		expect(users.id.defaultFn).toBeTypeOf('function')
	})

	// DV 2a, 2b: a point in time with its zone, without a precision.
	it('stores its times with their zone', () => {
		expect(users.createdAt.getSQLType()).toBe('timestamp with time zone')
		expect(users.adminDeactivatedAt.getSQLType()).toBe('timestamp with time zone')
	})

	it('allows one owner through a partial unique index (spec §3.4)', () => {
		const index = getTableConfig(users).indexes.find(
			candidate => candidate.config.name === 'users_one_owner',
		)!
		expect(index.config.unique).toBe(true)
		expect(index.config.where).toBeDefined()
	})
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the driver**

Run: `pnpm add pg@^8.23.1 && pnpm add -D @types/pg@^8.23.1` Expected: `package.json` gains `"pg": "^8.23.1"` in `dependencies` and `"@types/pg": "^8.23.1"` in `devDependencies` (the caret as `mysql2`'s `^3.24.3`; drizzle-orm 1.0.0-rc.4 declares `pg >=8` and `@types/pg *` as optional peers, `npm view drizzle-orm@1.0.0-rc.4 peerDependencies`). `mysql2` stays until Task 3 ports the e2e fixtures that import it (tsc covers `e2e/`). `pg` is on Next's default `serverExternalPackages` list (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/serverExternalPackages.md`), so `next.config.ts` does not change (DV 1a).

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the column helper and the enum types**

```ts
// db/schema/columns.ts
import { timestamp } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

/**
 * A point in time with its zone, as every hub table stores one (PostgreSQL wiki "Don't Do This": "Don't use timestamp
 * (without time zone)"; PostgreSQL 18 "Date/Time Types": "All timezone-aware dates and times are stored internally in
 * UTC"). No precision: "it rounds off the fractional part rather than truncating it" (the wiki's "Don't use
 * timestamp(0) or timestamptz(0)"), and a JS Date holds milliseconds anyway. Read as a Date (Drizzle "PostgreSQL column
 * types", timestamp, `mode: 'date'`). LobeHub's Drizzle schema keeps the same helper
 * (`lobehub/lobehub@canary:packages/database/src/schemas/_helpers.ts`).
 */
export const timestamptz = (name: string) => timestamp(name, { withTimezone: true, mode: 'date' })
```

```ts
// db/schema/enums.ts
import { pgEnum } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { ACCESS_MODES, MEMBERSHIP_SOURCES } from '@/lib/app-access'
import { ACCOUNT_SOURCES } from '@/lib/auth/account-source'
import { ROLES } from '@/lib/auth/roles'
import { SYNC_OUTCOMES, SYNC_TRIGGERS } from '@/lib/directory-status'

/*
 * The hub's static value sets as PostgreSQL enum types (Drizzle "PostgreSQL column types", enum; PostgreSQL 18
 * "Enumerated Types": enums are "primarily intended for static sets of values"; decision 24). Two columns are called
 * `source` and hold different sets, so each type is named for its set. drizzle-kit reads enum types from the schema's
 * exports, as Drizzle's example exports `moodEnum`, so db/schema/index.ts exports these with the tables. A value added
 * later through `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` "cannot be used until after the transaction has been committed" (PostgreSQL 18
 * "ALTER TYPE", Notes) and the migrator applies every pending migration in one transaction (DV 1d, 2e): a migration that
 * adds a value ships in a deploy where no migration uses it.
 */

export const userRole = pgEnum('user_role', ROLES)
export const accountSource = pgEnum('account_source', ACCOUNT_SOURCES)
export const accessMode = pgEnum('access_mode', ACCESS_MODES)
export const membershipSource = pgEnum('membership_source', MEMBERSHIP_SOURCES)
export const syncTrigger = pgEnum('sync_trigger', SYNC_TRIGGERS)
export const syncOutcome = pgEnum('sync_outcome', SYNC_OUTCOMES)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Port `db/schema/users.ts`**

Replace the whole file (68 lines, mysql-core) with:

```ts
// db/schema/users.ts
import { sql, type SQL } from 'drizzle-orm'
import {
	check,
	integer,
	pgTable,
	text,
	uniqueIndex,
	uuid,
	type AnyPgColumn,
} from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { generateUuidV4 } from '@/lib/helpers'

import { timestamptz } from './columns'
import { accountSource, userRole } from './enums'

/**
 * The expression the email index and every lookup by email share (Drizzle guide "Unique and Case-Insensitive Email
 * Handling", its `lower()` helper; PostgreSQL 18 "Indexes on Expressions": a unique index on `lower(col1)` "would
 * prevent creation of rows whose col1 values differ only in case"). A lookup puts it on both sides of the comparison
 * (PostgreSQL 18 "citext", Rationale). It folds case, not accents (ADR-0031).
 */
export const lower = (column: AnyPgColumn): SQL => sql`lower(${column})`

export const users = pgTable(
	'users',
	{
		id: uuid()
			.primaryKey()
			.$defaultFn(() => generateUuidV4()),
		name: text(),
		email: text().notNull(),
		/** The bcrypt hash of a local account; null for a directory account, whose password the directory checks (ADR-0029). */
		password: text(),
		/** ADR-0024: first run creates the owner; the owner manages admins and users, an admin manages users. */
		role: userRole().default('user').notNull(),
		sessionVersion: integer('session_version').default(0).notNull(),
		/** ADR-0027: set by an admin's Deactivate, cleared by Reactivate; the account is active while both markers are null. */
		adminDeactivatedAt: timestamptz('admin_deactivated_at'),
		/** The admin's users.id; no foreign key, so deleting that admin keeps the record. */
		adminDeactivatedBy: uuid('admin_deactivated_by'),
		/** ADR-0027: set and cleared by the directory sync and sign-in only (ADR-0029). */
		directoryDeactivatedAt: timestamptz('directory_deactivated_at'),
		/** ADR-0029: who owns the account's identity; a local account unless the directory sign-in made it. */
		source: accountSource().default('local').notNull(),
		/**
		 * The directory entry's key in canonical text (B3 spec §3.2: `objectGUID` as a lowercase GUID string, `entryUUID`
		 * lowercased), the only link to the entry (ADR-0026: never the email, the DN, the UPN or the login name).
		 */
		directoryId: text('directory_id'),
		/** The attribute that produced the key, so a change of LDAP_ID_ATTRIBUTE is detected (B3 spec §6.4 step 2). */
		directoryIdAttribute: text('directory_id_attribute'),
		/** The login attribute's value, for display and search. */
		directoryUsername: text('directory_username'),
		createdAt: timestamptz('created_at').defaultNow().notNull(),
		updatedAt: timestamptz('updated_at')
			.defaultNow()
			.notNull()
			.$onUpdate(() => new Date()),
	},
	table => [
		// Emails are unique whatever their case (Part 0 spec §3.4).
		uniqueIndex('users_email_key').on(lower(table.email)),
		// "By default, null values in a unique column are not considered equal, allowing multiple nulls in the column"
		// (PostgreSQL 18 "Unique Indexes"), so every local account can have none.
		uniqueIndex('users_directory_id_key').on(table.directoryId),
		// One owner (ADR-0024), held by the database: a unique index over the owner rows only (PostgreSQL 18 "Partial
		// Indexes", Example 11.3 "Setting up a Partial Unique Index"; Drizzle "Indexes & Constraints", `.where()`). Two
		// first runs at once both find the table empty; the second owner's insert is refused (23505), which
		// lib/data/setup.ts answers as setup done (Part 0 spec §3.4).
		uniqueIndex('users_one_owner')
			.on(table.role)
			.where(sql`${table.role} = 'owner'`),
		// "A check constraint is satisfied if the check expression evaluates to true or the null value" (PostgreSQL 18
		// "Check Constraints"), so `source` is NOT NULL and the other columns are tested with IS [NOT] NULL, which never
		// yields null. An `ldap` row also names the attribute that produced its key (B3 spec §3.2), which the sync's
		// `id_attribute_changed` stop reads for every `ldap` account (B3 spec §6.4 step 2).
		check(
			'users_source_credentials',
			sql`(${table.source} = 'local' AND ${table.password} IS NOT NULL AND ${table.directoryId} IS NULL) OR (${table.source} = 'ldap' AND ${table.password} IS NULL AND ${table.directoryId} IS NOT NULL AND ${table.directoryIdAttribute} IS NOT NULL)`,
		),
	],
)
```

```ts
// db/schema/apps.ts
import { boolean, bytea, jsonb, pgTable, text, uuid } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { generateUuidV4 } from '@/lib/helpers'

import { timestamptz } from './columns'
import { accessMode } from './enums'

export const difyApps = pgTable('dify_apps', {
	id: uuid()
		.primaryKey()
		.$defaultFn(() => generateUuidV4()),
	createdAt: timestamptz('created_at').defaultNow().notNull(),
	updatedAt: timestamptz('updated_at')
		.defaultNow()
		.notNull()
		.$onUpdate(() => new Date()),
	name: text().notNull(),
	/** A Dify app mode (lib/dify/types APP_MODES); kept free so a future mode does not break the row (charter §4.4). */
	mode: text(),
	description: text(),
	/**
	 * A JSON array of strings (PostgreSQL 18 "JSON Types": "most applications should prefer to store JSON data as
	 * jsonb"). Untyped on purpose (`unknown`, DV 8a): lib/data/apps.ts parseTags keeps the strings only.
	 */
	tags: jsonb(),
	isEnabled: boolean('is_enabled').default(true).notNull(),
	/** B3 spec §2 #9: a new app is closed until granted. */
	accessMode: accessMode('access_mode').default('restricted').notNull(),
	apiBase: text('api_base').notNull(),
	apiKey: text('api_key').notNull(),
	enableAnswerForm: boolean('enable_answer_form').default(false).notNull(),
	answerFormFeedbackText: text('answer_form_feedback_text'),
	enableUpdateInputAfterStarts: boolean('enable_update_input_after_starts')
		.default(false)
		.notNull(),
	openingStatementDisplayMode: text('opening_statement_display_mode'),
	enableAnnotation: boolean('enable_annotation').default(false).notNull(),
	// The Dify site icon, stored at create and sync time (charter §4.4): Dify's icon_url for an image expires.
	iconType: text('icon_type'),
	icon: text('icon'),
	iconBackground: text('icon_background'),
	/** PostgreSQL 18 "Binary Data Types" (DV 2c); Drizzle's bytea reads and writes a Buffer. */
	iconImage: bytea('icon_image'),
	iconMime: text('icon_mime'),
})
```

```ts
// db/schema/groups.ts
import { index, pgTable, primaryKey, text, uniqueIndex, uuid } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { generateUuidV4 } from '@/lib/helpers'

import { timestamptz } from './columns'
import { membershipSource } from './enums'
import { lower, users } from './users'

/**
 * Hub groups (B3 spec §3.1); `user_groups`, the name the MySQL schema gave them because MySQL reserves `groups`. Names
 * are unique whatever their case, as MySQL's case-insensitive collation kept them (`__tests__/group-management-actions.test.ts`
 * pins "Finance" against "finance"): a unique index on `lower(name)` (PostgreSQL 18 "Indexes on Expressions"; Part 0
 * Plan decision e). It folds case, not accents (ADR-0031).
 */
export const userGroups = pgTable(
	'user_groups',
	{
		id: uuid()
			.primaryKey()
			.$defaultFn(() => generateUuidV4()),
		name: text().notNull(),
		description: text(),
		createdAt: timestamptz('created_at').defaultNow().notNull(),
		updatedAt: timestamptz('updated_at')
			.defaultNow()
			.notNull()
			.$onUpdate(() => new Date()),
	},
	table => [uniqueIndex('user_groups_name_key').on(lower(table.name))],
)

/**
 * Memberships (B3 spec §2 #8): a person can be a member by hand and through the directory at once, one row per source;
 * an admin changes only `manual` rows and the sync only `directory` rows. Deleting the group or the account removes its
 * rows (PostgreSQL 18 "Foreign Keys", ON DELETE CASCADE). The primary key leads with `group_id`; `user_id` gets its own
 * index, which PostgreSQL does not create for a foreign key (Part 0 spec §3.2): an account's delete and the sign-in's
 * lookup by account read it.
 */
export const userGroupMembers = pgTable(
	'user_group_members',
	{
		groupId: uuid('group_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => userGroups.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		userId: uuid('user_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => users.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		source: membershipSource().notNull(),
		createdAt: timestamptz('created_at').defaultNow().notNull(),
	},
	table => [
		primaryKey({ columns: [table.groupId, table.userId, table.source] }),
		index('user_group_members_user_id_idx').on(table.userId),
	],
)
```

Replace the whole of `db/schema/app-grants.ts` (37 lines) with:

```ts
// db/schema/app-grants.ts
import { index, pgTable, primaryKey, uuid } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { difyApps } from './apps'
import { userGroups } from './groups'
import { users } from './users'

/*
 * App grants (B3 spec §3.1, ADR-0027): two typed tables rather than one with a principal-type column, so each grant
 * carries foreign keys and is removed with its app, group or account (Metabase relies on the same cascade; LibreChat
 * and Grafana, with a polymorphic column, delete orphans in code). A grant counts only while the app is restricted.
 * Each primary key leads with `app_id`; the other referencing column gets its own index, which PostgreSQL does not
 * create for a foreign key ("Foreign Keys": "it is often a good idea to index the referencing columns too"; Part 0 spec
 * §3.2), so a group's or an account's delete and the access rule's lookups by them use an index.
 */

export const appGroupGrants = pgTable(
	'app_group_grants',
	{
		appId: uuid('app_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => difyApps.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		groupId: uuid('group_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => userGroups.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
	},
	table => [
		primaryKey({ columns: [table.appId, table.groupId] }),
		index('app_group_grants_group_id_idx').on(table.groupId),
	],
)

export const appUserGrants = pgTable(
	'app_user_grants',
	{
		appId: uuid('app_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => difyApps.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		userId: uuid('user_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => users.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
	},
	table => [
		primaryKey({ columns: [table.appId, table.userId] }),
		index('app_user_grants_user_id_idx').on(table.userId),
	],
)
```

```ts
// db/schema/directory.ts
import { index, integer, pgTable, primaryKey, text, uniqueIndex, uuid } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { timestamptz } from './columns'
import { syncOutcome, syncTrigger } from './enums'
import { userGroups } from './groups'

/**
 * A hub group's link to a directory group, by the directory group's key (B3 spec §3.2, §6.5): renaming or moving the
 * group in the directory keeps the link. Deleting the hub group removes its links (ON DELETE CASCADE); the primary key
 * leads with `group_id`, which serves the foreign key. The name is what the groups page shows, refreshed by the sync;
 * `missing_since` is set while the sync does not find the group.
 */
export const userGroupDirectoryLinks = pgTable(
	'user_group_directory_links',
	{
		groupId: uuid('group_id')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => userGroups.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		directoryGroupId: text('directory_group_id').notNull(),
		directoryGroupName: text('directory_group_name').notNull(),
		missingSince: timestamptz('missing_since'),
	},
	table => [primaryKey({ columns: [table.groupId, table.directoryGroupId] })],
)

/**
 * One row per sync run (B3 spec §3.2, §6.4). `slot` is unique: a scheduled run's slot is its scheduled minute, so a
 * second container's claim of the same slot is skipped and that container skips the run (Documenso's deterministic slot
 * key). The run deletes rows older than 90 days. The id is the run's own uuid, made by the scheduler. `run_trigger` is
 * the name the MySQL schema gave the column, because MySQL reserves `trigger`.
 */
export const directorySyncRuns = pgTable(
	'directory_sync_runs',
	{
		id: uuid().primaryKey(),
		slot: text().notNull(),
		runTrigger: syncTrigger('run_trigger').notNull(),
		startedAt: timestamptz('started_at').notNull(),
		finishedAt: timestamptz('finished_at'),
		outcome: syncOutcome().default('running').notNull(),
		entriesSeen: integer('entries_seen').default(0).notNull(),
		deactivated: integer('deactivated').default(0).notNull(),
		reactivated: integer('reactivated').default(0).notNull(),
		updated: integer('updated').default(0).notNull(),
		conflicts: integer('conflicts').default(0).notNull(),
		groupErrors: integer('group_errors').default(0).notNull(),
		membershipsAdded: integer('memberships_added').default(0).notNull(),
		membershipsRemoved: integer('memberships_removed').default(0).notNull(),
		errorCode: text('error_code'),
	},
	table => [
		uniqueIndex('directory_sync_runs_slot_key').on(table.slot),
		index('directory_sync_runs_started_at_idx').on(table.startedAt),
	],
)
```

```ts
// db/schema/settings.ts
import { bytea, jsonb, pgTable, primaryKey, text, uuid } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core'

import { timestamptz } from './columns'

/**
 * Instance settings (settings spec §3.2): one JSON document per area (`branding`; later `smtp`, `ldap`), parsed by the
 * area's zod schema on every read and write. Stored as jsonb (PostgreSQL 18 "JSON Types": "most applications should
 * prefer to store JSON data as jsonb"); `.$type<unknown>()` is a type only (Drizzle "PostgreSQL column types", jsonb:
 * "it won't check runtime values"), so only a zod parse makes the value usable. A row is written only while the area's
 * mode is `database`. `updated_by` is the saving account's id with no foreign key, so deleting that account keeps the
 * record (as users.admin_deactivated_by, B3 spec §3.1).
 */
export const instanceSettings = pgTable('instance_settings', {
	area: text('area').primaryKey(),
	data: jsonb('data').$type<unknown>().notNull(),
	updatedAt: timestamptz('updated_at')
		.defaultNow()
		.notNull()
		.$onUpdate(() => new Date()),
	updatedBy: uuid('updated_by'),
})

/**
 * An area's files (spec §3.2): branding's processed PNGs, in the database like the app icons (dify_apps.icon_image),
 * so several hub containers need no shared folder. Deleting the area's row deletes them (PostgreSQL 18 "Foreign Keys",
 * ON DELETE CASCADE); the primary key leads with `area`, which serves the foreign key.
 */
export const instanceSettingFiles = pgTable(
	'instance_setting_files',
	{
		area: text('area')
			.notNull()
			.references(() => instanceSettings.area, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
		name: text('name').notNull(),
		bytes: bytea('bytes').notNull(),
		mime: text('mime').notNull(),
		updatedAt: timestamptz('updated_at')
			.defaultNow()
			.notNull()
			.$onUpdate(() => new Date()),
	},
	table => [primaryKey({ columns: [table.area, table.name] })],
)
```

Run: `git rm -r -q db/migrations && pnpm db:generate --name baseline` Expected: one new folder `db/migrations/<14 digits>_baseline/` with `migration.sql` and `snapshot.json`, and no question asked (an empty folder has nothing to rename; drizzle-kit 1.0.0-rc.4 does not prompt with a non-TTY stdin). `generate` reads the schema only and connects to no database, so no `DATABASE_URL` is needed (drizzle-kit's `prepareGenerateConfig` reads `dialect`, `schema`, `out`; checked in 1.0.0-rc.4 while planning). Git history keeps the MySQL migrations (decision 14).

```ts
// db/index.ts
import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/node-postgres'
import { Pool } from 'pg'

import { env } from '@/lib/env'
import { logActionError } from '@/lib/error-log'

/**
 * One pool per process (Drizzle "Get started with PostgreSQL", node-postgres: `drizzle({ client: pool })`; DV 1a). An
 * idle client whose connection the server ends, as a PostgreSQL restart does, makes the pool emit `error`; "if a pool
 * emits an error event and no listeners are added node will emit an uncaught error and potentially crash your node
 * process", and the client "will be automatically terminated and removed from the pool" (node-postgres "Pool", events;
 * DV 1b). The listener logs it by name and code (lib/error-log.ts). drizzle-orm 1.0.0-rc.4's config has no `schema`
 * option (the relational API is typed over defineRelations), and the DAL uses the query builder with explicit columns.
 */
const createDb = () => {
	// node-postgres waits for a new connection without a limit by default ("Pool": connectionTimeoutMillis "By default
	// this is 0 which means no timeout"). 10 s keeps the bound the hub had on MySQL (mysql2's `connectTimeout`, 10,000 ms
	// by default), so a database that does not answer fails a request after 10 s, as ADR-0030 records for the login
	// page, instead of holding it (Part 0 spec §1: no other change in behaviour).
	const pool = new Pool({ connectionString: env().databaseUrl, connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000 })
	pool.on('error', error => logActionError(error, 'Database pool'))
	return drizzle({ client: pool, logger: env().nodeEnv === 'development' })
}

export type Db = ReturnType<typeof createDb>

// In development the module is re-evaluated on edits, so the instance lives on globalThis (the inherited code did the
// same); in production the module loads once anyway.
const globalForDb = globalThis as unknown as { aiAppHubDb?: Db }

export const getDb = (): Db => (globalForDb.aiAppHubDb ??= createDb())
```

Replace the whole of `db/migrate.ts` (15 lines) with:

```ts
// db/migrate.ts
import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/node-postgres'
import { migrate } from 'drizzle-orm/node-postgres/migrator'
import { Pool } from 'pg'

const databaseUrl = process.env.DATABASE_URL
if (!databaseUrl) {
	console.error('The DATABASE_URL environment variable is missing; check the configuration')
	process.exit(1)
}

// Drizzle "Migrations", option 4: migrate() at runtime from the folder drizzle-kit generates, run by
// docker/entrypoint.sh before the server starts (decision 25). The pool is ended so the script leaves no connection
// behind; a failure rejects the top-level await, the process exits non-zero, and the entrypoint's `set -e` stops the
// container.
const pool = new Pool({ connectionString: databaseUrl })
await migrate(drizzle({ client: pool }), { migrationsFolder: './db/migrations' })
await pool.end()

console.log('Database migration complete')
process.exit(0)
```

`docker/entrypoint.sh` does not change: it still runs `node --experimental-strip-types db/migrate.ts`.

```ts
		const value: unknown = JSON.parse(text ?? '[]')

	}
}
```

```ts
/** The stored tags, a jsonb array (PostgreSQL 18 "JSON Types"): its strings only, and [] for anything else. */
```

```ts
export async function deleteApp(actor: SessionUser, id: string): Promise<boolean> {
	assertAdmin(actor)
	const [result] = await getDb().delete(difyApps).where(eq(difyApps.id, id))
	return result.affectedRows > 0
}
```

become:

```ts
export async function deleteApp(actor: SessionUser, id: string): Promise<boolean> {
	assertAdmin(actor)
	// node-postgres "Result": `rowCount` is the rows the command processed, null for a command that reports none.
	const result = await getDb().delete(difyApps).where(eq(difyApps.id, id))
	return (result.rowCount ?? 0) > 0
}
```

```ts
/** Deletes the group; its memberships, grants and (B3b) directory links go with it (ON DELETE CASCADE). */
export async function deleteGroup(actor: SessionUser, id: string): Promise<ActionResult> {
	assertAdmin(actor)
	const [result] = await getDb().delete(userGroups).where(eq(userGroups.id, id))
	return result.affectedRows > 0 ? ok(undefined) : fail('not_found')
}
```

become:

```ts
/** Deletes the group; its memberships, grants and (B3b) directory links go with it (ON DELETE CASCADE). */
export async function deleteGroup(actor: SessionUser, id: string): Promise<ActionResult> {
	assertAdmin(actor)
	// node-postgres "Result": `rowCount` is the rows the command processed, null for a command that reports none.
	const result = await getDb().delete(userGroups).where(eq(userGroups.id, id))
	return (result.rowCount ?? 0) > 0 ? ok(undefined) : fail('not_found')
}
```

`lib/data/settings.ts` lines 140–145:

```ts
// An upsert updates the row in place (Drizzle "Upsert"; MySQL 8.4 "INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE
// Statement"), where REPLACE would delete it and cascade away the file rows that belong to it.
await tx
	.insert(instanceSettings)

	.onDuplicateKeyUpdate({ set: { data, updatedAt: now, updatedBy: actor.id } })
```

become:

```ts
// An upsert updates the row in place (Drizzle "Upsert", `onConflictDoUpdate`; PostgreSQL 18 "INSERT": ON
// CONFLICT DO UPDATE "updates the existing row that conflicts with the row proposed for insertion"), where a
// delete and insert would cascade away the file rows that belong to it. pg-core has no onDuplicateKeyUpdate.
await tx
	.insert(instanceSettings)
	.values({ area, data, updatedAt: now, updatedBy: actor.id })
	.onConflictDoUpdate({
		target: instanceSettings.area,
		set: { data, updatedAt: now, updatedBy: actor.id },
	})
```

```ts
/**
 * One account's refreshed directory fields (spec §6.4 step 3). A new email the unique index refuses (1062: another
 * account has it) is dropped and the other fields written: the row keeps its old email and the run counts a conflict
 * (spec §2 #11, decision ac). `updated` says the write matched the account: mysql2 connects with `FOUND_ROWS` by
 * default, so `affectedRows` is the rows the WHERE matched, not only those it changed (MySQL 8.4 C API
 * "mysql_affected_rows()": with CLIENT_FOUND_ROWS "the affected-rows value is the number of rows 'found'"). An account
 * deleted during the run matches nothing, and is counted neither updated nor in conflict.
 */
```

```ts
		name: 'logo',
		bytes: logo.bytes,
		mime: 'image/png',
	}),
	{ set: expect.objectContaining({ bytes: logo.bytes, mime: 'image/png' }) },
])
```

with:

```ts
// ON CONFLICT names the key that decides "the same row" (PostgreSQL 18 "INSERT": conflict_target): the area for the
// document, the area and name for a file, so the upsert updates the row in place and keeps its file rows.
expect(calls[2]).toEqual([
	'upsert',
	'instance_settings',
	expect.objectContaining({ area: 'branding', data: { name: 'Acme' }, updatedBy: 'a1' }),
	{
		target: instanceSettings.area,
		set: expect.objectContaining({ data: { name: 'Acme' }, updatedBy: 'a1' }),
	},
])
expect(calls[3]).toEqual([
	'upsert',
	'instance_setting_files',
	expect.objectContaining({
		area: 'branding',
		name: 'logo',
		bytes: logo.bytes,
		mime: 'image/png',
	}),
	{
		target: [instanceSettingFiles.area, instanceSettingFiles.name],
		set: expect.objectContaining({ bytes: logo.bytes, mime: 'image/png' }),
	},
])
```

(Vitest's equality takes the same column object as equal before it compares fields, `Object.is` first, so the target pins the very columns the DAL passes.)

with:

```ts
const state = {
	writes: [] as Write[],
	reads: [] as Read[],
	rows: [] as unknown[],
	/** node-postgres "Result": `rowCount` is the rows the statement matched, or null. */
	rowCount: 1 as number | null,

}
```

Lines 40–45, replace:

```ts
const result = () => {
	const failure = state.failures.shift()
	return failure ? Promise.reject(failure) : Promise.resolve([{ affectedRows: state.affectedRows }])
}
```

with:

```ts
// A write without .returning() resolves to node-postgres's QueryResult, an object (node_modules/drizzle-orm/
// node-postgres/session.d.ts, NodePgQueryResultHKT), not mysql2's [ResultSetHeader, fields] array.
const result = () => {
	const failure = state.failures.shift()
	return failure ? Promise.reject(failure) : Promise.resolve({ rowCount: state.rowCount })
}
```

- [ ] **Step 24: Port `__tests__/data-apps.test.ts`, the tags as a jsonb value**

`dify_apps.tags` is `jsonb` now: node-postgres hands back the parsed value (PostgreSQL 18 "JSON Types"; Drizzle `jsonb` maps a string from the driver through `JSON.parse` and passes anything else through), so `parseTags` receives an array or `null`, never JSON text.

Line 1, replace `import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/mysql2'` with:

```ts
import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/node-postgres'
```


with:

```ts

```

Lines 53–60, replace:

```ts
describe('parseTags', () => {
	it('reads a JSON array of strings and tolerates anything else', () => {
		expect(parseTags('["a","b"]')).toEqual(['a', 'b'])
		expect(parseTags('["a",1]')).toEqual(['a'])
		expect(parseTags('nope')).toEqual([])
		expect(parseTags(null)).toEqual([])
	})
})
```

with:

```ts
describe('parseTags', () => {
	// The jsonb column's value as the driver parsed it (PostgreSQL 18 "JSON Types"): only its strings are tags.
	it('reads an array of strings from the jsonb value and tolerates anything else', () => {
		expect(parseTags(['a', 'b'])).toEqual(['a', 'b'])
		expect(parseTags(['a', 1])).toEqual(['a'])
		expect(parseTags('["a"]')).toEqual([])
		expect(parseTags({ tags: ['a'] })).toEqual([])
		expect(parseTags(null)).toEqual([])
	})
})
```

### Task 3: PostgreSQL in Compose and the e2e harness on pg

**Files:**

- Test: `__tests__/compose-files.test.ts` (new)
- Modify: `docker-compose.local.yml` (rewritten), `docker-compose.e2e.yml` (lines 1–27: header and the `mysql` service; lines 35 and 84: container names), `docker-compose.yml` (rewritten: the hub's own production template), `docker-compose.dev.yml` (names only)
- Modify: `.env.template` (lines 3–4), `.env.e2e` (line 5), `playwright.config.ts` (lines 1, 29, 32)
- Modify: `e2e/global-setup.ts` (lines 7–19), `e2e/fixtures/db.ts` (rewritten), `e2e/auth.setup.ts` (lines 2, 32–61), `e2e/fixtures/users.ts` (lines 13, 31–44, 49, 64–70, 74–82), `e2e/fixtures/access.ts` (lines 3, 26–89), `e2e/fixtures/directory.ts` (lines 3, 12, 17–19, 23, 26–43), `e2e/fixtures/branding.ts` (lines 7–13)
- Modify: `e2e/admin-apps.spec.ts` (lines 9, 22–29, 119, 342–363), `e2e/admin-groups.spec.ts` (lines 2, 97, 118–123), `e2e/admin-users.spec.ts` (lines 2, 11–21, 133, 147), `e2e/auth.spec.ts` (lines 84, 87–92, 118–121), `e2e/chat-files.spec.ts` (lines 254–262), `e2e/directory-groups.spec.ts` (lines 2, 57–63), `e2e/app-access.spec.ts` (lines 20–22)
- Modify: `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` (`mysql2` removed)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: Task 2's PostgreSQL schema, `drizzle.config.ts` with `dialect: 'postgresql'` (so `pnpm exec drizzle-kit migrate` applies the baseline), `pg` and `@types/pg` in `package.json`.
- Produces: the Compose service name `postgres` in every file; local project `ai-app-hub-local` (containers `ai-app-hub-local-postgres`, `ai-app-hub-local-app`, volume `pgdata`, PostgreSQL on `127.0.0.1:5432`); e2e project `ai-app-hub-e2e` (containers `ai-app-hub-e2e-postgres`, `ai-app-hub-e2e-ldap-ad`, `ai-app-hub-e2e-ldap-openldap`; PostgreSQL on `127.0.0.1:5433`, tmpfs, credentials `e2e`/`e2e`/`e2e`); production template project `ai-app-hub` on network `ai-app-hub-network`; `.env.e2e` `DATABASE_URL=postgres://e2e:e2e@127.0.0.1:5433/e2e`; `e2e/fixtures/db.ts` `export const withDb = async <T>(fn: (db: Client) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>` (pg `Client`); e2e raw SQL through `db.query(text, values)` with `$1…` placeholders; the fixtures' `LIKE` are `ILIKE`. Task 4's `db` project starts the same `postgres` service of `docker-compose.e2e.yml`.

Nothing in this task starts `docker-compose.local.yml`: the owner's local MySQL stack (`dify-app-hub-local`) keeps running untouched until Task 14 asks the owner to change `.env` and take it down. The error predicates still test MySQL's codes until Task 5 and the email lookups compare with case until Task 6, so this task runs only the two specs that need neither; the whole suite runs in Tasks 11 and 14.

- [ ] **Step 1: Take down the old e2e stack while its file still names it**

The old file's project is `dify-app-hub-e2e`; once Step 5 renames it, Compose no longer finds those containers, and the old `ldap-ad` would keep ports 10636 and 10389. This is CLAUDE.md's documented reset ("`docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml --profile ldap down -v` resets it and the test directories"). Stop `pnpm dev` and any `pnpm test:e2e` first.

Run: `docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml --profile ldap down -v` Expected: the containers `dify-app-hub-e2e-mysql`, `dify-app-hub-e2e-ldap-ad` and `dify-app-hub-e2e-ldap-openldap` (whichever were running) and the network `dify-app-hub-e2e_default` are removed. `docker ps --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -c dify-app-hub-e2e` then prints `0`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test**

```ts
// __tests__/compose-files.test.ts
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'

/*
 * The PostgreSQL services as the compose files declare them (Part 0 spec §2), pinned as text, as the baseline's SQL is
 * pinned in __tests__/db-baseline.test.ts. Compose itself validates the files (`docker compose config --quiet`, Task 3);
 * these cases pin the values the image's docs require, which a slip would break without an error.
 */
const read = (file: string) => readFileSync(file, 'utf8')

describe.each(['docker-compose.local.yml', 'docker-compose.e2e.yml', 'docker-compose.yml'])(
	'%s',
	file => {
		const text = read(file)

		it('runs PostgreSQL 18 under the service name postgres', () => {
			expect(text).toMatch(/^ {2}postgres:\n {4}image: postgres:18\n/m)
		})

		// docker-library postgres, "PGDATA": the 18 images declare VOLUME /var/lib/postgresql; a mount at
		// /var/lib/postgresql/data applies to 17 and below only.
		it('keeps the data at /var/lib/postgresql, never at the path of 17 and below', () => {
			expect(text).toMatch(/^ {6}- (pgdata:)?\/var\/lib\/postgresql$/m)
			expect(text).not.toContain('/var/lib/postgresql/data')
		})

		// The image's docs, "Caveats": the default 64 MB /dev/shm can fail with "could not resize shared memory segment".
		it('gives the server 128 MB of shared memory', () => {
			expect(text).toMatch(/^ {4}shm_size: 128mb$/m)
		})

		// Compose "Control startup order" example, with -h: the first-start server listens on the socket only (DV 7d).
		it('reports healthy only once the server answers on TCP', () => {
			expect(text).toContain(
				"test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}']",
			)
		})

		it('names neither MySQL nor the old product', () => {
			expect(text.toLowerCase()).not.toContain('mysql')
			expect(text).not.toContain('dify-app-hub')
		})
	},
)

describe.each(['docker-compose.local.yml', 'docker-compose.yml'])('%s', file => {
	const text = read(file)

	// Compose "Control startup order": `condition: service_healthy` waits for the healthcheck to pass.
	it('starts the hub only once PostgreSQL is healthy', () => {
		expect(text).toMatch(/depends_on:\n {6}postgres:\n {8}condition: service_healthy/)
	})

	it('keeps the data in the named volume pgdata', () => {
		expect(text).toMatch(/^ {6}- pgdata:\/var\/lib\/postgresql$/m)
		expect(text).toMatch(/^volumes:\n {2}pgdata:/m)
	})
})

describe('docker-compose.e2e.yml', () => {
	const text = read('docker-compose.e2e.yml')

	it('keeps the throwaway database in tmpfs, published on 127.0.0.1:5433 with the test credentials', () => {
		expect(text).toMatch(/^ {4}tmpfs:\n {6}- \/var\/lib\/postgresql$/m)
		expect(text).toContain("- '127.0.0.1:5433:5432'")
		expect(text).toMatch(/POSTGRES_DB: e2e\n {6}POSTGRES_USER: e2e\n {6}POSTGRES_PASSWORD: e2e\n/)
	})
})

describe('docker-compose.dev.yml', () => {
	it("carries the hub's names", () => {
		const text = read('docker-compose.dev.yml')
		expect(text).toContain('container_name: ai-app-hub-self-deploy')
		expect(text).toContain('ai-app-hub-network')
		expect(text).not.toContain('dify-app-hub')
	})
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Run it to see it fail**

Run: `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit __tests__/compose-files.test.ts` Expected: FAIL (every file still runs `mysql:8.4`, publishes 3306/3307 and names `dify-app-hub`; the dev file names `dify-app-hub-self-deploy`).

- [ ] **Step 4: Rewrite `docker-compose.local.yml`**

The whole file (today's 47 lines run `mysql:8.4` as `dify-app-hub-local-mysql` on `127.0.0.1:3306` with the volume `mysql-data`):

```yaml
# Local test stack: builds the hub from this checkout and runs it with its own PostgreSQL server.
# Usage: docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml up -d --build
# Settings and passwords are read from .env (not committed). POSTGRES_DB, POSTGRES_USER and POSTGRES_PASSWORD create
# the database on the volume's first start (the postgres image's docs, "Environment Variables"); the hub reaches it
# with DATABASE_URL=postgres://<user>:<password>@postgres:5432/<db>.
#
# Quick dev loop (no image build): stop the app container, keep PostgreSQL, run `pnpm dev`
# on the host against 127.0.0.1:5432 (DATABASE_URL in .env.development.local). See CLAUDE.md.
name: ai-app-hub-local

services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:18
    container_name: ai-app-hub-local-postgres
    environment:
      # Compose "Interpolation": ${VAR:?error} stops with the message when the variable is unset or empty; the image
      # requires a non-empty POSTGRES_PASSWORD.
      POSTGRES_DB: ${POSTGRES_DB:?Set POSTGRES_DB in .env}
      POSTGRES_USER: ${POSTGRES_USER:?Set POSTGRES_USER in .env}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?Set POSTGRES_PASSWORD in .env}
    ports:
      # This machine only, for `pnpm dev` on the host; not reachable from the network
      - '127.0.0.1:5432:5432'
    volumes:
      # The 18 images declare VOLUME /var/lib/postgresql and keep PGDATA at /var/lib/postgresql/18/docker below it; the
      # older mount at its `data` folder applies to 17 and below only (the postgres image's docs, "PGDATA").
      - pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql
    # The default 64 MB /dev/shm can fail with "could not resize shared memory segment" (the image's docs, "Caveats").
    shm_size: 128mb
    healthcheck:
      # Compose "Control startup order" example. -h 127.0.0.1: during first-run initialisation the server listens on
      # the Unix socket only, so a TCP check reports ready only once the real server is up (the image's docs).
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 30s
    restart: unless-stopped

  app:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    container_name: ai-app-hub-local-app
    ports:
      - '5300:5300'
    env_file:
      - .env
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  pgdata:
```

```yaml

…
```

with:

```yaml
# Throwaway services for the tests, published on 127.0.0.1 only. PostgreSQL (tmpfs) is started by e2e/global-setup.ts;
# the two LDAP test directories (profile `ldap`) are started by name: ldap-ad by e2e/global-setup.ts, both by
# __tests__/ldap/global-setup.ts (pnpm test:ldap). Stop them with:
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml down             (PostgreSQL)
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml down -v ldap-ad ldap-openldap
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml --profile ldap down -v   (everything)
name: ai-app-hub-e2e

services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:18
    container_name: ai-app-hub-e2e-postgres
    environment:
      POSTGRES_DB: e2e
      POSTGRES_USER: e2e
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: e2e
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:5433:5432'
    # The 18 images' VOLUME (the postgres image's docs, "PGDATA"), in memory: every new container starts empty.
    tmpfs:
      - /var/lib/postgresql
    # The default 64 MB /dev/shm can fail with "could not resize shared memory segment" (the image's docs, "Caveats").
    shm_size: 128mb
    healthcheck:
      # Compose "Control startup order" example, with -h 127.0.0.1 (the first-start server listens on the socket only).
      # start_interval checks every second during start_period, so `up --wait` returns soon after the server is ready
      # (Compose file reference, healthcheck `start_interval`, as the LDAP services below use it).
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 30s
      start_interval: 1s
```

Then line 35, `    container_name: dify-app-hub-e2e-ldap-ad`, becomes `    container_name: ai-app-hub-e2e-ldap-ad`, and line 84, `    container_name: dify-app-hub-e2e-ldap-openldap`, becomes `    container_name: ai-app-hub-e2e-ldap-openldap`. Nothing else in the LDAP services changes (spec §2).

- [ ] **Step 6: Rewrite `docker-compose.yml` as the hub's own production template**

The whole file (today's 19 lines are upstream's: `lexmin0412/dify-app-hub:latest` with an external MySQL URL and Chinese comments):

```yaml
# AI App Hub, production template: the hub and its own PostgreSQL 18, usable without the source tree.
# Put a .env file beside this one (see .env.template in the repository) with at least POSTGRES_DB, POSTGRES_USER,
# POSTGRES_PASSWORD, DATABASE_URL=postgres://<user>:<password>@postgres:5432/<db>, NEXTAUTH_SECRET and NEXTAUTH_URL.
# Start: DOCKERHUB_USERNAME=<the Docker Hub account that publishes the image> docker compose up -d
# The hub migrates the database when its container starts (docker/entrypoint.sh). Run one hub container per database
# until migrations move to a one-shot service (Part 0 spec §1, decision 25).
name: ai-app-hub

services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:18
    container_name: ai-app-hub-postgres
    environment:
      # Compose "Interpolation": ${VAR:?error} stops with the message when the variable is unset or empty.
      POSTGRES_DB: ${POSTGRES_DB:?Set POSTGRES_DB in .env}
      POSTGRES_USER: ${POSTGRES_USER:?Set POSTGRES_USER in .env}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?Set POSTGRES_PASSWORD in .env}
    volumes:
      # The 18 images declare VOLUME /var/lib/postgresql (the postgres image's docs, "PGDATA").
      - pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql
    # The default 64 MB /dev/shm can fail with "could not resize shared memory segment" (the image's docs, "Caveats").
    shm_size: 128mb
    healthcheck:
      # Compose "Control startup order" example, with -h 127.0.0.1 (the first-start server listens on the socket only).
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 30s
    restart: unless-stopped
    networks:
      - ai-app-hub-network

  app:
    # The image docker-bake.hcl publishes (`${DOCKERHUB_USERNAME}/ai-app-hub`).
    image: ${DOCKERHUB_USERNAME:?Set DOCKERHUB_USERNAME to the Docker Hub account that publishes the image}/ai-app-hub:latest
    container_name: ai-app-hub
    ports:
      - '5300:5300'
    env_file:
      - .env
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
    restart: unless-stopped
    networks:
      - ai-app-hub-network

networks:
  ai-app-hub-network:
    driver: bridge

volumes:
  pgdata:
```

`name: ai-app-hub` fixes the project name, so the volume is `ai-app-hub_pgdata` whatever the folder is called (Compose file reference, top-level `name`: "the project name"; volumes are prefixed with it).

- [ ] **Step 7: Rename `docker-compose.dev.yml`'s names**

Line 11, `    container_name: dify-app-hub-self-deploy`, becomes `    container_name: ai-app-hub-self-deploy`. Lines 19 and 23, `      - dify-app-hub-network` and `  dify-app-hub-network:`, become `      - ai-app-hub-network` and `  ai-app-hub-network:`. Its comments stay as upstream wrote them (spec §2: "gets the new names").

- [ ] **Step 8: Run the test to see it pass**

Run: `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit __tests__/compose-files.test.ts` Expected: PASS (21 tests).

- [ ] **Step 9: Let Compose validate the four files**

Compose reads `.env` for interpolation, and the shell's variables win over it (Compose "Set environment variables", precedence), so stand-in values make the check independent of the owner's `.env`, which still holds the MySQL keys until Task 14. `--quiet` prints nothing but errors (Compose CLI `docker compose config`: "Only validate the configuration, don't print anything"), so no value from `.env` reaches the terminal.

Run:

```bash
POSTGRES_DB=hub POSTGRES_USER=hub POSTGRES_PASSWORD=check docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml config --quiet
docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml config --quiet
POSTGRES_DB=hub POSTGRES_USER=hub POSTGRES_PASSWORD=check DOCKERHUB_USERNAME=example docker compose -f docker-compose.yml config --quiet
docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml config --quiet
```

Expected: no output and exit status 0 for each. Then, as a check of a `:?` message: `env -u DOCKERHUB_USERNAME POSTGRES_DB=hub POSTGRES_USER=hub POSTGRES_PASSWORD=check docker compose -f docker-compose.yml config --quiet` fails with "Set DOCKERHUB_USERNAME to the Docker Hub account that publishes the image" (as long as `.env` does not set `DOCKERHUB_USERNAME`).
