- [ ] **Step 7: Claim the slot with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING**

In `lib/data/directory.ts`, replace the comment and the function at lines 491–518 (base commit), which after Task 5 read:

```ts
/**
 * Claims a run's slot (spec §6.4 "Claim"): the unique key refuses a second insert for the same slot (23505), and that
 * caller skips. Documenso builds a deterministic id per cron slot so that racing instances "collide on the primary key
 * instead of creating duplicates" (`documenso@38ecb217:packages/lib/jobs/client/local.ts:19-22`); GoodJob and Solid
 * Queue keep a unique index per scheduled time (`ad-and-reference-projects.md` B.9).
 */
export async function claimSyncRun(run: {
	id: string
	slot: string
	trigger: SyncTrigger
	startedAt: Date
}): Promise<boolean> {
	try {
		await reissueOnDeadlock(() =>
			getDb().insert(directorySyncRuns).values({
				id: run.id,
				slot: run.slot,
				runTrigger: run.trigger,
				startedAt: run.startedAt,
				outcome: 'running',
			}),
		)
		return true
	} catch (error) {
		if (isUniqueViolation(error, 'directory_sync_runs_slot_key')) return false
		throw error
	}
}
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * Claims a run's slot (spec §6.4 "Claim") with one insert that skips on the slot's unique index and gives back the id
 * (PostgreSQL 18 "INSERT": "ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING simply avoids inserting a row as its alternative action", and
 * RETURNING answers "each row actually inserted"; Drizzle "Insert", `onConflictDoNothing({ target })`; DV 6). A row
 * back is the claim; none means another container holds the slot, and that caller skips. A refusal of anything else
 * propagates. Documenso builds a deterministic id per cron slot so that racing instances "collide on the primary key
 * instead of creating duplicates" (`documenso@38ecb217:packages/lib/jobs/client/local.ts:19-22`); GoodJob and Solid
 * Queue keep a unique index per scheduled time (`ad-and-reference-projects.md` B.9).
 */
export async function claimSyncRun(run: {
	id: string
	slot: string
	trigger: SyncTrigger
	startedAt: Date
}): Promise<boolean> {
	const claimed = await reissueOnDeadlock(() =>
		getDb()
			.insert(directorySyncRuns)
			.values({
				id: run.id,
				slot: run.slot,
				runTrigger: run.trigger,
				startedAt: run.startedAt,
				outcome: 'running',
			})
			.onConflictDoNothing({ target: directorySyncRuns.slot })
			.returning({ id: directorySyncRuns.id }),
	)
	return claimed.length > 0
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Insert the memberships with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING**

Replace the comment and the function at lines 389–438 (base commit), which after Tasks 2 and 5 read:

```ts
/**
 * Applies the planned `directory` memberships per hub group (spec §2 #8: manual rows untouched). An insert refused as a
 * duplicate (a sign-in added the row meanwhile) or a missing reference (the group or the account was deleted during
 * the run) is retried row by row: a duplicate is already there; a missing reference marks its hub group, and
 * `groupErrors` counts the hub groups so marked, once each (spec §6.4 step 4 counts an error per group).
 */
export async function applyMembershipChanges(
	plan: Pick<MembershipPlan, 'add' | 'remove'>,
): Promise<{ added: number; removed: number; groupErrors: number }> {
	const db = getDb()
	let added = 0
	let removed = 0
	const failedGroups = new Set<string>()
	for (const [groupId, rows] of Map.groupBy(plan.remove, row => row.groupId))
		for (const part of batches(rows.map(row => row.userId))) {
			const result = await reissueOnDeadlock(() =>
				db
					.delete(userGroupMembers)
					.where(
						and(
							eq(userGroupMembers.groupId, groupId),
							eq(userGroupMembers.source, 'directory'),
							inArray(userGroupMembers.userId, part),
						),
					),
			)
			removed += result.rowCount ?? 0
		}
	const insert = (groupId: string, userIds: readonly string[]) =>
		reissueOnDeadlock(() => db.insert(userGroupMembers).values(directoryRows(groupId, userIds)))
	for (const [groupId, rows] of Map.groupBy(plan.add, row => row.groupId))
		for (const part of batches(rows.map(row => row.userId))) {
			try {
				await insert(groupId, part)
				added += part.length
			} catch (error) {
				if (!isUniqueViolation(error) && !isForeignKeyViolation(error)) throw error
				for (const userId of part) {
					try {
						await insert(groupId, [userId])
						added += 1
					} catch (rowError) {
						if (isForeignKeyViolation(rowError)) failedGroups.add(groupId)
						else if (!isUniqueViolation(rowError)) throw rowError
					}
				}
			}
		}
	return { added, removed, groupErrors: failedGroups.size }
}
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * Applies the planned `directory` memberships per hub group (spec §2 #8: manual rows untouched). An insert skips a row
 * that is there already, such as one a sign-in added meanwhile (PostgreSQL 18 "INSERT": with no conflict target,
 * "conflicts with all usable constraints (and unique indexes) are handled", here the primary key; Drizzle "Insert",
 * `onConflictDoNothing()`; DV 8), so `added` is the rows the server inserted (node-postgres `rowCount`, the command
 * tag's count). A missing reference (23503: the group or the account was deleted during the run) refuses the whole
 * batch, which is retried row by row: the rows whose parents remain are inserted, and each refused row marks its hub
 * group; `groupErrors` counts the hub groups so marked, once each (spec §6.4 step 4 counts an error per group).
 */
export async function applyMembershipChanges(
	plan: Pick<MembershipPlan, 'add' | 'remove'>,
): Promise<{ added: number; removed: number; groupErrors: number }> {
	const db = getDb()
	let added = 0
	let removed = 0
	const failedGroups = new Set<string>()
	for (const [groupId, rows] of Map.groupBy(plan.remove, row => row.groupId))
		for (const part of batches(rows.map(row => row.userId))) {
			const result = await reissueOnDeadlock(() =>
				db
					.delete(userGroupMembers)
					.where(
						and(
							eq(userGroupMembers.groupId, groupId),
							eq(userGroupMembers.source, 'directory'),
							inArray(userGroupMembers.userId, part),
						),
					),
			)
			removed += result.rowCount ?? 0
		}
	const insert = (groupId: string, userIds: readonly string[]) =>
		reissueOnDeadlock(() =>
			db.insert(userGroupMembers).values(directoryRows(groupId, userIds)).onConflictDoNothing(),
		)
	for (const [groupId, rows] of Map.groupBy(plan.add, row => row.groupId))
		for (const part of batches(rows.map(row => row.userId))) {
			try {
				added += (await insert(groupId, part)).rowCount ?? 0
			} catch (error) {
				if (!isForeignKeyViolation(error)) throw error
				for (const userId of part) {
					try {
						added += (await insert(groupId, [userId])).rowCount ?? 0
					} catch (rowError) {
						if (!isForeignKeyViolation(rowError)) throw rowError
						failedGroups.add(groupId)
					}
				}
			}
		}
	return { added, removed, groupErrors: failedGroups.size }
}
```

`isUniqueViolation` stays imported: `recordDirectorySignIn` and `refreshDirectoryAccount` still use it.

- [ ] **Step 9: Rewrite the deadlock and batch comments for PostgreSQL**

In `lib/data/directory.ts`, the `DEADLOCK_ATTEMPTS` comment (lines 203–211 at the base commit) ends with:

```ts
 * with a growing delay (`directus@8e140f94:api/src/utils/transaction.ts:29-50`). No delay: Prisma's and Ghost's run
 * again at once, and MySQL's guidance names none ("Deadlocks are not dangerous. Just try again.").
 */
```

Replace those three lines with:

```ts
 * with a growing delay (`directus@8e140f94:api/src/utils/transaction.ts:29-50`). No delay: Prisma's and Ghost's run
 * again at once, and PostgreSQL names none. It ends a deadlock by "aborting one of the transactions involved, allowing
 * the other(s) to complete" (PostgreSQL 18 "Explicit Locking", 13.3.4 Deadlocks), reports SQLSTATE 40P01
 * `deadlock_detected` (Appendix A), and says "It may also be advisable to retry deadlock failures" ("Serialization
 * Failure Handling", 13.5).
 */
```

Replace the batch comment (lines 252–257 at the base commit):

```ts
/**
 * Decision ae: at most 1,000 ids per statement, each its own statement. Drizzle's mysql2 session sends a query through
 * mysql2's `query`, which fills the values in on the client (mysql2 docs; not a prepared `execute`), so a statement is
 * bounded by `max_allowed_packet` (64 MB by default, MySQL 8.4 "Packet Too Large"); 1,000 ids also stay under the
 * 65,535 placeholders of a prepared statement (ER_PS_MANY_PARAM, 1390).
 */
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * Decision ae: at most 1,000 ids per statement, each its own statement. node-postgres sends the query text and the
 * values apart, the values "safely substituted into the query ... within the server itself" (node-postgres "Queries",
 * Parameterized query; `node_modules/pg/lib/query.js` `requiresPreparation`: a query with values goes as Parse and
 * Bind), and Bind counts the values in a 16-bit field (PostgreSQL 18 "Message Formats", Bind: "Int16 The number of
 * parameter values that follow"), so one statement takes at most 65,535 of them. 1,000 ids, or 1,000 membership rows of
 * three values each, stay well under that.
 */
```

Replace the `reissueOnDeadlock` comment (lines 264–271 at the base commit):

```ts
/**
 * Runs one of the sync's statements, and again when InnoDB rolled it back as a deadlock's victim (1213), up to
 * DEADLOCK_ATTEMPTS attempts in all. Under autocommit each statement "forms a single transaction on its own" (MySQL 8.4
 * "autocommit, Commit, and Rollback"), and a deadlock rolls the whole transaction back ("InnoDB Error Handling"), so
 * the statement runs again from a clean state: "Always be prepared to re-issue a transaction if it fails due to
 * deadlock" ("How to Minimize and Handle Deadlocks"). The last attempt's deadlock propagates: the run fails, and the
 * next run completes the reconciliation, which is idempotent (spec §6.4 "Claim"). Any other error is not re-issued.
 */
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * Runs one of the sync's statements, and again when PostgreSQL ended it as a deadlock's victim (40P01), up to
 * DEADLOCK_ATTEMPTS attempts in all. Outside a transaction block each statement "has an implicit BEGIN and (if
 * successful) COMMIT wrapped around it" (PostgreSQL 18 "Transactions", 3.4), and the deadlock aborts that transaction
 * ("Explicit Locking", 13.3.4), so the statement runs again from a clean state ("It may also be advisable to retry
 * deadlock failures", "Serialization Failure Handling", 13.5). The last attempt's deadlock propagates: the run fails,
 * and the next run completes the reconciliation, which is idempotent (spec §6.4 "Claim"). Any other error is not
 * re-issued.
 */
```

### Task 9: UUID checks on every id from outside

Spec §3.2: every id column is a PostgreSQL `uuid` now, and PostgreSQL refuses a string that is not a UUID with SQLSTATE `22P02 invalid_text_representation` (PostgreSQL 18 Appendix A) where MySQL's text column matched nothing. Every id that comes from outside is therefore checked before it reaches a query, and a malformed one is answered as an unknown one. The spots, checked against the code:

- the shared route guard `resolveDifyRoute` (`lib/dify/route.ts`), which all 25 route files under `app/api/dify/[appId]/` call (`grep -L resolveDifyRoute $(find app/api/dify -name route.ts)` prints nothing; the spec's "26" is one too many);
- the chat page `app/(user)/chat/[appId]/page.tsx`;
- the icon route `app/api/apps/[appId]/icon/route.ts`;
- the admin actions: the app actions already check with a local `isId` (z.uuid), the group actions with `groupIdSchema` (z.uuid); the five user actions, the groups' `memberIds` and the apps' `access.userIds` go through `userIdSchema`, today `z.string().min(1).max(36)` (B2 decision h), which becomes the UUID check;
- the directory groups route (`app/api/directory/groups/route.ts`) takes no id (only `?q=`), so nothing is added there.

Ids from the session token are the hub's own and need no check (spec §3.2). Task 5's `22P02` safety net stays behind these checks.

**Files:**

- Create: `lib/ids.ts`
- Modify: `lib/dify/route.ts` (lines 4, 28–43: the import, the doc comment, the app read), `app/(user)/chat/[appId]/page.tsx` (lines 4, 6–14), `app/api/apps/[appId]/icon/route.ts` (lines 6, 20–21), `app/(admin)/user-management/schemas.ts` (lines 4, 22–26), `app/(admin)/group-management/schemas.ts` (lines 4, 53–54), `app/(admin)/app-management/schemas.ts` (lines 5, 14, 17), `app/(admin)/app-management/actions.ts` (lines 4, 10, 20)
- Test: `__tests__/ids.test.ts` (new); `__tests__/dify-route.test.ts`, `__tests__/chat-page.test.ts`, `__tests__/app-icon-route.test.ts`, `__tests__/dify-routes-app.test.ts`, `__tests__/dify-routes-app-upstream.test.ts`, `__tests__/dify-routes-chat.test.ts`, `__tests__/dify-routes-files.test.ts`, `__tests__/dify-routes-run.test.ts`, `__tests__/user-management-actions.test.ts`, `__tests__/user-management-rank.test.ts`, `__tests__/user-management-schemas.test.ts`, `__tests__/group-management-actions.test.ts`, `__tests__/group-management-schemas.test.ts`, `__tests__/app-management-actions.test.ts`, `__tests__/app-management-schemas.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `difyErrorResponse(code, message, status)` (`lib/dify/errors.ts`), `fail('not_found')` (`lib/action-result.ts`), `<AppUnavailable reason="missing" />`; Task 5's error fixtures in `__tests__/group-management-actions.test.ts` and `__tests__/app-management-actions.test.ts` (this task touches only the ids in those files).
- Produces: `lib/ids.ts` (client-safe): `export const idSchema = z.uuid()` and `export const isId = (value: string): boolean`. `userIdSchema = idSchema` and `groupIdSchema = idSchema` keep their names, so `app/(admin)/user-management/actions.ts` and `app/(admin)/group-management/actions.ts` do not change. `accessSchema.groupIds` is `z.array(idSchema)`, `accessSchema.userIds` and `groupInputSchema.memberIds` are arrays of `userIdSchema`. `resolveDifyRoute` answers `404 { code: 'app_not_found', message: 'No such app.', status: 404 }` for `!isId(appId)` after the session check and before `getAppAccess`; the chat page renders `<AppUnavailable reason="missing" />` for `!isId(appId)` without `getChatApp`; the icon route answers `404 icon_not_found` for `!isId(appId)` after the session check, without `getAppIcon`. The UUID constant in the tests is `'3b241101-e2bb-4255-8caf-4136c566a962'`.

What `z.uuid()` takes (zod 4.6.5, measured with the repo's `node_modules/zod`; zod 4 API "UUIDs": "Validates RFC 9562/4122 compliant UUID strings … Use `z.guid()` to validate UUID-like identifiers without RFC byte constraints"): the version digit 1–8 and the variant digit 8, 9, a or b, either case, plus the nil and max UUIDs. `crypto.randomUUID()`, the e2e app ids (`e2e00000-0000-4000-8000-00000000000N`) and the constant above pass. That is a subset of what PostgreSQL's `uuid` input takes (PostgreSQL 18 "8.12. UUID Type": "upper-case digits, the standard format surrounded by braces, omitting some or all hyphens, adding a hyphen after any group of four digits"), so an id that passes the check never raises `22P02`.

```ts
	it('takes the ids the hub generates and the seeded e2e ids', () => {
		expect(isId(crypto.randomUUID())).toBe(true)
		expect(isId('e2e00000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001')).toBe(true)
		expect(isId('3b241101-e2bb-4255-8caf-4136c566a962')).toBe(true)

			expect(isId(id)).toBe(false)
	})

	// takes braces and missing hyphens (PostgreSQL 18 "8.12. UUID Type"): an id that passes never raises 22P02.
	it('is stricter than PostgreSQL: the alternative input forms are refused too', () => {

		expect(idSchema.safeParse('legacy-id').success).toBe(false)
	})
})
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the id check**

```ts
// lib/ids.ts
import * as z from 'zod'

/**
 * Every id the hub stores is a UUID (ADR-0031: `uuid` columns since the fresh PostgreSQL start, filled by
 * `crypto.randomUUID()`). An id that comes from outside, a URL segment or an action argument, is checked here before it
 * reaches a query: PostgreSQL refuses a malformed one with SQLSTATE 22P02 invalid_text_representation (PostgreSQL 18
 * Appendix A) where MySQL's text column matched nothing (spec §3.2). `z.uuid()` takes RFC 9562/4122 UUIDs only (zod 4
 * "UUIDs"), a subset of what PostgreSQL's `uuid` input accepts (PostgreSQL 18 "8.12. UUID Type": upper-case digits,
 * braces, hyphens left out), so an id that passes never raises 22P02. Client-safe: the admin schemas use it.
 */
export const idSchema = z.uuid()

/** Whether an id from outside can be one of the hub's; a malformed one is answered as an unknown one (spec §3.2). */
export const isId = (value: string): boolean => idSchema.safeParse(value).success
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Check the id in the route guard**

In `lib/dify/route.ts`, add the import after line 4 (`import { getAppAccess } from '@/lib/data/apps'`):

```ts
import { isId } from '@/lib/ids'
```

Replace lines 28–43:

```ts
/**
 * The first three steps of every Dify handler (charter §4.1): the session first, so a revoked token learns nothing
 * about apps; then the app; then its credentials, which never leave the server. Each refusal is the envelope, a
 * failure of the session or app read too (a lost database, an EnvError): a logged 500 envelope, not Next's empty one.
 */
export const resolveDifyRoute = async (
	params: Promise<{ appId: string }>,
): Promise<ResolvedDifyRoute> => {
	try {
		const actor = await verifySession()
		if (!actor)
			return { ok: false, response: difyErrorResponse('unauthorized', 'Sign in required.', 401) }
		const { appId } = await params
		const app = await getAppAccess(actor, appId)
		if (!app)
			return { ok: false, response: difyErrorResponse('app_not_found', 'No such app.', 404) }
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * The first three steps of every Dify handler (charter §4.1): the session first, so a revoked token learns nothing
 * about apps; then the app; then its credentials, which never leave the server. An id that is not a UUID is an unknown
 * app, answered before the app read (spec §3.2: PostgreSQL refuses it with 22P02; lib/ids.ts). Each refusal is the
 * envelope, a failure of the session or app read too (a lost database, an EnvError): a logged 500 envelope, not Next's
 * empty one.
 */
export const resolveDifyRoute = async (
	params: Promise<{ appId: string }>,
): Promise<ResolvedDifyRoute> => {
	try {
		const actor = await verifySession()
		if (!actor)
			return { ok: false, response: difyErrorResponse('unauthorized', 'Sign in required.', 401) }
		const { appId } = await params
		const app = isId(appId) ? await getAppAccess(actor, appId) : null
		if (!app)
			return { ok: false, response: difyErrorResponse('app_not_found', 'No such app.', 404) }
```

```tsx
 * is not a UUID is a missing app and reaches no query (spec §3.2; lib/ids.ts).

export default async function AppChatPage({ params }: PageProps<'/chat/[appId]'>) {

	const { appId } = await params
```

- [ ] **Step 2: The users DAL**

In `lib/data/users.ts`, replace the `lockTarget` comment (lines 178–183):

```ts
/**
 * A locking read of one account by its primary key (decision d; MySQL "Locking Reads": SELECT … FOR UPDATE). A
 * concurrent change to the same account, such as the owner promoting a user an admin is editing, waits and is then
 * read, so the rank is never checked against a stale role. One row, for the few milliseconds the transaction lasts;
 * consistent reads such as sign-in do not wait.
 */
```

with:

```ts
/**
 * A locking read of one account by its primary key (decision d; PostgreSQL 18 "SELECT", The Locking Clause: SELECT …
 * FOR UPDATE). A concurrent change to the same account, such as the owner promoting a user an admin is editing, waits
 * and is then read: at READ COMMITTED "it is the updated version of the row that is locked and returned" ("Transaction
 * Isolation", 13.2.1), so the rank is never checked against a stale role. One row, for the few milliseconds the
 * transaction lasts; plain reads such as sign-in do not wait ("reading never blocks writing and writing never blocks
 * reading", "Concurrency Control", 13.1).
 */
```

In the `changeOwnPassword` comment, replace lines 403–407:

```ts
 * The check is tied to the row it overwrites (decision d). A wrong password is refused on the plain read, before any
 * lock. The write then reads the row again with a locking read on its primary key (MySQL 8.4 "Locking Reads": "the
 * regular SELECT statement does not give enough protection. Other transactions can update or delete the same rows
 * you just queried") and goes ahead only while the row still holds the hash that was verified. A password the owner
 * or an admin set in between is not overwritten: the change is refused as a wrong current password.
```

with:

```ts
 * The check is tied to the row it overwrites (decision d). A wrong password is refused on the plain read, before any
 * lock. The write then reads the row again with a locking read on its primary key, which keeps other transactions from
 * changing it "until the current transaction ends" (PostgreSQL 18 "Explicit Locking", 13.3.2 Row-Level Locks; the plain
 * read takes no such lock), and goes ahead only while the row still holds the hash that was verified. A password the
 * owner or an admin set in between is not overwritten: the change is refused as a wrong current password.
```

`ECONNRESET` is Node's code for a connection the peer closed (Node "Errors", Common system errors), what node-postgres surfaces when the server drops a socket. These tests only need "some other failure": none asserts the code's text.

**Files:**

- Modify: `e2e/auth.spec.ts` (a new case after line 39, inside `test.describe('signed out', …)`)
- Modify: `e2e/directory-sign-in.spec.ts` (a new case after line 108, inside `test.describe('answers every refusal with the same message (spec §7.3)', …)`)
- Modify: `e2e/admin-users.spec.ts` (a new case inside `test.describe('user CRUD', …)`, after the case "editing a user onto an email another account holds says it is in use and writes nothing")
- Create: `e2e/malformed-ids.spec.ts`, `e2e/database.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `withDb` (`e2e/fixtures/db.ts`, a pg `Client`, Task 3); `seedUser`, `deleteUsersLike` (whose pattern is matched with ILIKE, Task 3), `chooseLocalAccount`, `openUsers`, `signInWithDirectory` (`e2e/fixtures/users.ts`); `DIRECTORY_PASSWORD`, `directoryAccount`, `directoryAccountsByUsername` (`e2e/fixtures/directory.ts`); `e2eEnv` (`e2e/fixtures/env.ts`); the index `users_one_owner` (Task 2); `isUniqueViolation(error, 'users_email_key')` → `email_in_use` (Task 5); `emailMatches` in the sign-in, the users DAL and the directory DAL (Task 6); `isId` in the route guard, the chat page and the icon route (Task 9).
- Produces: nothing new for later tasks; these cases are the browser-level proof of Review Focus 1, 4 and 5.

These cases are acceptance tests for Tasks 2, 5, 6 and 9, written after them, so they pass at once: a failure here is a defect in those tasks (fix it there, test-first), not a reason to loosen a case. What each case depends on:

| Case | Depends on |
| --- | --- |
| (a) local sign-in with the email in another case | Task 6 (`findAccount` compares `lower()` on both sides); Task 2 (the `lower(email)` index) |
| (b) a local account's email in another case refuses a directory entry's first sign-in | Task 6 (the directory DAL's `emailTakenBy`); without it the insert meets `users_email_key` twice and the sign-in fails with an error instead of the refusal |
| (c) the users page refuses an email that differs only in case | Task 2 (the index), Task 5 (23505 on `users_email_key` → `email_in_use`), Task 6 (the DAL's check finds it before the insert) |
| (d) a malformed id answers as an unknown one | Task 9 (`isId` before any query); Task 5's 22P02 net is the fallback for the routes |
| (e) the database refuses a second owner | Task 2 (`users_one_owner`) |

- [ ] **Step 1: (a) Sign in with the email typed in another case**

In `e2e/auth.spec.ts`, insert after line 39 (the closing `})` of the case "signing in honours the callbackUrl the proxy added"):

```ts
// ADR-0031: the users_email_key index is on lower(email) and the sign-in compares lower() on both sides, so the
// email typed in another case finds the account, as MySQL's case-insensitive collation did (Part 0 spec §3.4).
test('signing in with the email typed in another case lands on /apps', async ({ page }) => {
	await page.goto('/login')
	await chooseLocalAccount(page)
	await page.getByLabel('Email').fill(e2eEnv.E2E_ADMIN_EMAIL.toUpperCase())
	await page.getByLabel('Password').fill(e2eEnv.E2E_ADMIN_PASSWORD)
	await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Log in' }).click()
	await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/apps$/)
})
```

(`ADMIN@E2E.LOCAL` passes the login form's antd `type: 'email'` rule (`components/auth/login-form.tsx`, line 126), whose pattern takes upper-case letters; nothing on the client lower-cases it, so the server receives it as typed.)

- [ ] **Step 4: (d) Malformed ids answer as unknown ones**

A spec of its own rather than a case in `e2e/app-access.spec.ts`: that spec is about grants and signs a user-role account in against restricted apps it seeds; here the only variable is the id's format, signed in as the owner (the projects' storage state), who may use every app, so nothing but the id decides the answer.

```ts
// e2e/malformed-ids.spec.ts
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto'

import { expect, test } from '@playwright/test'

/*
 * Part 0 spec §3.2 (ADR-0031): ids are PostgreSQL uuids, and the server refuses a malformed one with 22P02 instead of
 * matching nothing. Every id from a URL is checked first (lib/ids.ts), so a malformed id answers exactly as an unknown
 * one: the chat page's "missing" Result and the routes' 404 envelope, never a 500. Signed in as the owner (the
 * project's storage state), who may use every app.
 */
const missing = 'No Dify app configuration found. Contact your administrator.'
const notFound = { code: 'app_not_found', message: 'No such app.', status: 404 }

const cases = [
	['a malformed id', 'abc'],
	['an unknown uuid', randomUUID()],
] as const

for (const [label, id] of cases) {
	test.describe(label, () => {
		test('the chat page shows the missing app', async ({ page }) => {
			const response = await page.goto(`/chat/${id}`)
			expect(response?.status()).toBeLessThan(500)
			await expect(page.getByText(missing)).toBeVisible()
		})

		test('the Dify routes answer the 404 envelope before any request to Dify', async ({ page }) => {
			const parameters = await page.request.get(`/api/dify/${id}/parameters`)
			expect(parameters.status()).toBe(404)
			expect(await parameters.json()).toEqual(notFound)
			// The guard runs before the body is read (session → app → role → input, lib/dify/route.ts).
			const send = await page.request.post(`/api/dify/${id}/chat-messages`, {
				data: { query: 'hello', inputs: {} },
			})
			expect(send.status()).toBe(404)
			expect(await send.json()).toEqual(notFound)
		})

		test('the icon route answers 404', async ({ page }) => {
			const icon = await page.request.get(`/api/apps/${id}/icon`)
			expect(icon.status()).toBe(404)
			expect(await icon.json()).toMatchObject({ code: 'icon_not_found', status: 404 })
		})
	})
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: (e) The database refuses a second owner**

```ts
// e2e/database.spec.ts
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto'

import { expect, test } from '@playwright/test'

import { withDb } from './fixtures/db'

test.describe('the single owner (ADR-0031)', () => {
	test.use({ storageState: { cookies: [], origins: [] } })

	/*
	 * PostgreSQL 18 "Partial Indexes", Example 11.3: users_one_owner is unique on role where role = 'owner', so the
	 * database itself refuses a second owner, whichever path writes it. On this database the first-run path never
	 * reaches the insert (an account exists, so createOwner answers `forbidden` first); the concurrent first runs are
	 * proven against an empty database in __tests__/db/owner.db.test.ts (Task 7). The case touches the shared database
	 * only and opens no page: the three projects use one database one after another (workers: 1), so it runs once.
	 */
	test('refuses a second owner at the database', async () => {
		test.skip(
			test.info().project.name !== 'desktop-light',
			'a database-only case runs in one project',
		)
		const email = `second-owner-${randomUUID()}@e2e.local`
		try {
			const insert = withDb(db =>
				db.query(
					"INSERT INTO users (id, name, email, password, role) VALUES ($1, 'Second owner', $2, 'not-a-hash', 'owner')",
					[randomUUID(), email],
				),
			)
			// node-postgres rejects with its DatabaseError, which carries the SQLSTATE and the index's name (PostgreSQL 18
			// Appendix A: 23505 unique_violation).
			await expect(insert).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: '23505', constraint: 'users_one_owner' })
		} finally {
			await withDb(db => db.query('DELETE FROM users WHERE email = $1', [email]))
		}
	})

	// Part 0 spec §6: the first-run page is closed on a set-up database; its server redirect answers 307 to /login.
	test('/init answers 307 to /login once an owner exists', async ({ request }) => {
		const response = await request.get('/init', { maxRedirects: 0 })
		expect(response.status()).toBe(307)
		expect(response.headers().location).toMatch(/\/login$/)
	})
})
```

(Playwright `test.skip(condition, description)` inside a test skips it conditionally; `request.get` with `maxRedirects: 0` returns the redirect itself instead of following it, Playwright `APIRequestContext.get`.)

The smblds container copies the certificate and runs the seed once per provisioning (`10-tls.sh`, `20-seed.sh`, guarded by a marker on its tmpfs), and OpenLDAP reads its TLS files at bootstrap, so the running containers keep the old CA until they are recreated (`docker compose down` removes the containers; `-v` their anonymous volumes; `--profile ldap` includes the two directories, Docker "Using profiles with Compose"). This also stops the e2e `postgres`, which the next run's global setup starts again on an empty tmpfs (the setup project then runs `/init`).

```bash

pnpm test:ldap
```

Expected: the directory suite passes against both directories over LDAPS and StartTLS with the new `ca.crt` (`__tests__/ldap/servers.ts` reads `e2e/fixtures/ldap/tls/ca.crt`); its teardown stops them again.

```markdown
---
status: proposed
date: 2026-10-11
decision-makers: LovingCivilian (fork owner)
consulted: Claude Code session (provider-hub brainstorm, Part 0 spec and plan)
---

# Move the database to PostgreSQL 18

## Context and Problem Statement

[ADR-0004](0004-keep-mysql-through-drizzle.md) kept MySQL 8.4 through Drizzle because a port would have touched upstream-owned backend files on every sync. [ADR-0022](0022-treat-the-overhaul-line-as-fully-fork-owned.md) took that reason away: this line is fork-owned and takes upstream by cherry-pick only. ADR-0004's note of 2026-10-09 records the owner's wish to move to PostgreSQL, which their other projects and Dify on the production server run.

The provider-hub programme (charter `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-11-provider-hub-charter.md`) turns the hub into "AI App Hub", a front for AI apps of several providers. Its Parts 1 to 3 store provider settings and secrets as `jsonb` and keep a message store, so the owner put the move first, as Part 0 (running record `docs/superpowers/research/2026-10-11-provider-hub/README.md`, decision 13), with a fresh start (decision 14: no hub data, local or production, has to survive), in the hub's own container (decision 15), on the design approved as decision 16 and corrected by the documentation check `doc-verification.md` (decision 23; rows cited below as "DV 1a" and so on), with migrations still run by the container's entrypoint (decision 25) and `uuid` ids (decision 27).

The question: which PostgreSQL, in which container, through which driver and column types; how the MySQL-specific behaviours the code relies on are re-derived; and how the move is proven.

## Decision Drivers

- Documented approaches only ([ADR-0002](0002-use-documented-library-approaches-only.md)); every piece below was checked against its docs in `doc-verification.md`.
- Parts 1 to 3 are written for PostgreSQL: `jsonb` documents, partial unique indexes, `ON CONFLICT`.
- The hub's upgrades, backups and restarts stay independent of Dify's (decision 15).
- No data has to survive (decision 14), so there is no data migration to write or test.
- One engine across the dev loop, the Docker stack, the e2e suite and a live test of the data layer.
- One production container today; several containers later (`CLAUDE.md`, "Deployment").
- No other change in behaviour, apart from the ones recorded here (spec §1).

## Considered Options

Where the database runs:

- **PostgreSQL 18 in the hub's own container** (chosen). The current major, 18.6, supported to 2030-11-14 (postgresql.org/support/versioning, checked 2026-10-11).
- **The PostgreSQL that Dify already runs on the production server.**
- **Stay on MySQL 8.4** (ADR-0004).

The driver:

- **`pg` (node-postgres) through `drizzle-orm/node-postgres`** (chosen).
- **`postgres` (postgres.js) through `drizzle-orm/postgres-js`.**

The data:

- **A fresh start with one baseline migration** (chosen).
- **Copy the MySQL data across.**

Ids:

- **PostgreSQL's `uuid` type, with every id from outside checked first** (chosen).
- **`text` ids**, as MySQL's `varchar(36)`.

Case-insensitive email:

- **A unique index on `lower(email)`, and `lower()` on both sides of every lookup** (chosen).
- **The `citext` extension.**
- **A nondeterministic collation.**

The single owner:

- **A partial unique index on `role` where `role = 'owner'`** (chosen).
- **B2's locking read under REPEATABLE READ**, as on MySQL.

Migrations:

- **Each container migrates at start, in its entrypoint** (chosen for now).
- **A one-shot `migrate` service the app services wait on.**

## Decision Outcome

Chosen: PostgreSQL 18 in the hub's own container, reached through `pg` and `drizzle-orm/node-postgres`, from one baseline migration on an empty database, with `uuid` ids, an expression index for case-insensitive emails, a partial unique index for the single owner, and the entrypoint migrating at start. Every MySQL-specific behaviour the code relied on is re-derived in its documented PostgreSQL form, each with a test, and a live test project proves the ones a mock cannot.

### The container

- Every compose file that runs a database has a `postgres` service on `postgres:18`; the MySQL services are gone. Credentials `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (required, non-empty), `POSTGRES_USER`, `POSTGRES_DB` (the image's docs, DV 7a).
- The volume is mounted at `/var/lib/postgresql` (`pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql`): the 18 images moved `VOLUME` there and keep `PGDATA` at `/var/lib/postgresql/18/docker`; the old `/var/lib/postgresql/data` mount applies to 17 and below (DV 7b).
- `shm_size: 128mb`: the default 64 MB `/dev/shm` can fail with "could not resize shared memory segment" (the image's "Caveats", DV 7c).
- Healthcheck `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d $${POSTGRES_DB}` with Docker's documented timings (`interval: 10s`, `timeout: 10s`, `retries: 5`, `start_period: 30s`); `-h` waits for TCP, because the first-start server listens on the Unix socket only (DV 7d). The hub waits on `condition: service_healthy` (Compose "Control startup order").
- `docker-compose.local.yml`: project `ai-app-hub-local`, PostgreSQL published on `127.0.0.1:5432` only. `docker-compose.e2e.yml`: project `ai-app-hub-e2e`, PostgreSQL on `127.0.0.1:5433` with `tmpfs` at `/var/lib/postgresql`, credentials `e2e`/`e2e`/`e2e`. `docker-compose.yml`, the production template, becomes the hub's own: the app plus `postgres` on the network `ai-app-hub-network`.

### The driver and the connection

- `pg` ^8.23.1 with `@types/pg` ^8.23.1; `drizzle-orm` and `drizzle-kit` `1.0.0-rc.4`, exact (npm dist-tag `rc`, its release notes checked); `mysql2` removed. Drizzle documents both `pg` and postgres.js; its notes favour `pg` here (per-query type parsers; postgres.js prepares statements by default, which "you may need to opt out of"), and its get-started guide uses `pg` (DV 1a). `pg` is in Next's default `serverExternalPackages`, so `next.config.ts` is unchanged.
- `db/index.ts` builds `new Pool({ connectionString })`, adds `pool.on('error', …)`, then `drizzle({ client: pool, logger })`, kept on `globalThis.aiAppHubDb`. node-postgres: an idle client whose server goes away emits `error` through the pool, the client "will be automatically terminated and removed from the pool", and with no listener Node "will emit an uncaught error and potentially crash your node process" (node-postgres "Pool", events; DV 1b). The listener logs the error by name and code.
- `db/migrate.ts` runs `migrate()` from `drizzle-orm/node-postgres/migrator`; `drizzle.config.ts` says `dialect: 'postgresql'`; both use Drizzle's default log `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` (DV 1c, 1d).

### Column types

| MySQL | PostgreSQL | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `datetime(3)` with `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(3)` | `timestamp({ withTimezone: true, mode: 'date' }).defaultNow()`, no precision | DV 2a; DV 2b (PostgreSQL wiki "Don't Do This": a precision rounds rather than truncates) |
| `varchar(n)` | `text`; a length limit lives in zod (ADR-0025) | DV 11 (wiki: "Don't use varchar(n) by default") |
| `varchar(36)` ids | `uuid`, still generated by the app (`crypto.randomUUID()`), so an id is known before the insert | DV 11 (PostgreSQL "UUID Type") |
| `mediumblob` | `bytea` | DV 2c |
| `dify_apps.tags` (text holding JSON), `instance_settings.data` (`json`) | `jsonb` | DV 2d ("most applications should prefer to store JSON data as `jsonb`") |
| `mysqlEnum` | `pgEnum`: `user_role`, `account_source`, `access_mode`, `membership_source`, `sync_trigger`, `sync_outcome`, in `db/schema/enums.ts` and exported from the schema module, where drizzle-kit collects them | DV 2e; Drizzle "PostgreSQL column types", enum |
| `boolean`, `int`, CHECKs, composite primary keys, foreign keys with cascade | the same in pg-core | Drizzle's pg column types |

The table names stay (`dify_apps` until Part 1). PostgreSQL does not index the referencing columns of a foreign key, which InnoDB did (PostgreSQL "Constraints", Foreign Keys; MySQL 8.4 "FOREIGN KEY Constraints"; DV 12), so three indexes are added: `app_group_grants_group_id_idx`, `app_user_grants_user_id_idx`, `user_group_members_user_id_idx`. Every other foreign key's column is the leading column of its table's primary key.

### Migrations

`db/migrations/` was emptied (git keeps the MySQL history) and `drizzle-kit generate` wrote one baseline, `db/migrations/<timestamp>_baseline/`, applied to an empty database by `drizzle-kit migrate` and by the entrypoint. AGENTS.md's rule holds: generate and migrate, never `push`.

### Re-derived MySQL behaviours

| MySQL behaviour the code relied on | PostgreSQL form | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Case-insensitive emails through the column's collation | `uniqueIndex('users_email_key').on(lower(t.email))`; every lookup through `emailMatches(email)` (`lib/data/email.ts`), `lower(users.email) = lower($1)`, in the sign-in, the users DAL, the directory DAL and the forgot-password route | DV 3a–3c (PostgreSQL "Indexes on Expressions" and "citext": "you always have to remember to use `lower` on both the column and the query value"; Drizzle guide "Unique and Case-Insensitive Email Handling") |
| The single owner through B2's gap lock under REPEATABLE READ | ``uniqueIndex('users_one_owner').on(t.role).where(sql`role = 'owner'`)``; `createOwner` runs at the default READ COMMITTED and answers any `23505` as `forbidden` | DV 4 (PostgreSQL "Partial Indexes", Example 11.3); DV 7 (REPEATABLE READ adds `40001` failures callers "must be prepared to retry") |
| `ER_DUP_ENTRY` 1062, `ER_NO_REFERENCED_ROW_2` 1452, `ER_LOCK_DEADLOCK` 1213 | `isUniqueViolation(error, constraint?)` (`23505`, reading the error's `constraint` where a table has several unique indexes), `isForeignKeyViolation` (`23503`), `isDeadlock` (`40P01`), `isInvalidTextRepresentation` (`22P02`), on the driver error under Drizzle's `DrizzleQueryError.cause` | DV 5a, 5b (PostgreSQL "Error Codes": "Applications that need to know which error condition has occurred should usually test the error code") |
| mysql2's `affectedRows` with `FOUND_ROWS` | `(result.rowCount ?? 0) > 0`; PostgreSQL counts the matched rows | DV 5c (node-postgres "Result"; PostgreSQL "UPDATE", Outputs) |
| `onDuplicateKeyUpdate` | `onConflictDoUpdate({ target, set })` | DV 8 (Drizzle "Upsert"; PostgreSQL "INSERT", ON CONFLICT) |
| The sync's slot claim, an insert a unique key refuses | `onConflictDoNothing({ target: slot }).returning({ id })`; no row back means "skip" | DV 6 |
| The sync's membership batches with a row-by-row fallback on duplicates | `onConflictDoNothing()`; a missing parent (`23503`) is still retried row by row and marks its group | DV 8 |
| Comments on gap locks, `FOUND_ROWS`, `max_allowed_packet` and InnoDB | rewritten for PostgreSQL; the 1,000-id batches stay, under PostgreSQL's 65,535 parameters per statement (the Bind message counts them in an Int16) | DV 7 (PostgreSQL "Message Formats") |
| A caught error inside a transaction | nothing changes: every catch sits outside its transaction; a future catch inside one needs a nested `tx.transaction()` (a savepoint) | DV 5d |

### Ids from outside

PostgreSQL's `uuid` type refuses a string that is not a UUID with `22P02 invalid_text_representation` instead of matching nothing (DV 11). Every id from outside is therefore checked with `idSchema = z.uuid()` (`lib/ids.ts`) before it reaches a query: in `resolveDifyRoute` (all 25 `app/api/dify/[appId]/…` route files), the chat page, the icon route, and the admin actions and their schemas for apps, users and groups (`userIdSchema` and `groupIdSchema` are `idSchema`; ADR-0024 decision h, "account ids are not checked as UUIDs", ends with the fresh start, since every id is now the hub's own UUID). A malformed id answers as an unknown one: 404 `app_not_found`, `icon_not_found` or the action's `not_found`. As a safety net, a `22P02` that reaches `toActionFailure` or `errorResponseFrom` is logged and answered as not found, never as a 500. Ids from the session token are the hub's own and are not checked. The directory groups route takes no id (`?q=` only).

### Errors and logs

`describeError` (`lib/error-log.ts`) logs a `DrizzleQueryError` and a bare pg `DatabaseError` by name and SQLSTATE code only: the driver error's `detail` carries the refused value (an email in `Key (lower(email))=(…) already exists.`), a `22P02` message carries the malformed input, and `DrizzleQueryError` carries the query's parameters. MySQL's `errno` is gone; Node's own socket errors keep their `errno`.

### Tests

- Unit tests (Vitest, node environment) on `drizzle-orm/node-postgres`'s `drizzle.mock()`: the rendered SQL of the locking reads, the email lookups and the conflict paths; the error predicates; the id checks; the `rowCount` consumers with `null`; the schema's DDL (`__tests__/db-baseline.test.ts` and the schema tests).
- A live test project, `pnpm test:db` (`__tests__/db/*.db.test.ts`), a Vitest project of its own like `pnpm test:ldap` ([ADR-0029](0029-sign-directory-accounts-in-over-ldap-and-keep-them-in-step-with-a-scheduled-sync.md)): it starts the e2e compose's `postgres`, recreates and migrates the database `hub_dal`, and runs the data layer against it: the `lower(email)` index, two concurrent first runs leaving one owner, the error predicates on real driver errors, the email lookups in another case, the slot claim and the membership batches. A mock cannot prove an index or a conflict.
- The e2e suite on the three projects against a throwaway PostgreSQL ([ADR-0010](0010-verify-the-frontend-with-playwright-and-a-stub-dify-api.md)), with new cases: signing in with the email in another case (local and directory), an account whose email differs only in case refused, a second owner refused by the partial index, and a malformed app id answering as an unknown app.
- The Docker gate: the migration applied to an empty volume, `/init` on the fresh database, and the hub surviving `docker compose restart postgres`.

### Behaviour changes, recorded

- **Email matching ignores case but no longer ignores accents.** MySQL's `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` collation treated `josé@example.com` and `jose@example.com` as one address; `lower()` does not (spec §3.4). `citext` and nondeterministic collations were not chosen (below).
- **Group names, the same.** The collation also kept group names unique without case (B3a deviation 2, pinned by `e2e/admin-groups.spec.ts`). `user_groups_name_key` is a unique index on `lower(name)`, so "Finance" and "finance" are still one name, and "Équipe" and "Equipe" are now two (a gap in the spec, closed while planning: spec §1 promises no other change).
- **Two first saves of a settings area at once: the later one wins.** MySQL's gap lock queued the second first save behind the first, which then built on the first's document. PostgreSQL locks only rows a read returns, so before the area's row exists both saves build from no document and the upsert keeps the later one (`lib/data/settings.ts`, `writeInstanceSetting`). Once the row exists, saves queue on its lock as before. Found while planning; the spec does not mention it.
- **The connection's wait is kept.** node-postgres's pool waits for a connection without a limit by default (`connectionTimeoutMillis`: "By default this is 0 which means no timeout", node-postgres "Pool"); the hub sets `connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000`, the 10 s mysql2 used (`db/index.ts`), so a database that does not answer still fails a request after 10 s ([ADR-0030](0030-set-each-settings-area-from-the-environment-or-the-admin-page.md)'s login-page note).
- **The fresh start.** First-run setup runs again, and the apps, groups, grants and settings are added again by hand (spec §5).

### Corrections to the spec's text, found while planning

- Spec §3.2 counts 26 `[appId]` route files; there are 25 (24 for the 27 Dify operations, plus `files/remote`), all through `resolveDifyRoute`.
- Spec §3.2 lists "the directory groups route, where it takes an id": it takes none.
- Spec §4 leaves out `.github/workflows/deploy-docs.yml`, which filters on the docs package's name and is renamed with it.
- Spec §7 says `docs/auth-gate.md` describes MySQL's case-insensitivity; it did not. It held the MySQL owner-password recovery command and "MySQL error 3819", which change, and it gains the case-and-accents limit above. `docs/ldap.md` held "MySQL error 3819" too.

### Consequences

- Good, because Parts 1 to 3 can store provider settings, secrets and messages as `jsonb`, and the database enforces what MySQL left to the application: the UUID format, one owner, case-insensitive email uniqueness.
- Good, because the hub's database is its own: upgrading, backing up or restarting Dify does not touch it.
- Good, because a database restart no longer risks the hub process: the pool's listener logs the error and the pool replaces the client.
- Good, because the live test project proves the indexes, the conflict paths and the error codes against a real server.
- Bad, because every id from outside needs its `z.uuid()` check; a missed one is caught as `22P02`, logged and answered as not found.
- Bad, because a value added to a PostgreSQL enum cannot be used in the transaction that adds it, and Drizzle's migrator runs every pending migration in one transaction (DV 1d, DV 2e; PostgreSQL "ALTER TYPE", Notes). A migration that adds an enum value ships in a deploy where no migration uses that value, and a value cannot be removed short of re-creating the type. The static sets stay enums (decision 24); Part 1's `apps.provider` is `text` with a CHECK.
- Bad, because `migrate()` takes no lock: two hub containers starting together race, and the loser fails `set -e` and restarts (decision 25). Before running several containers, migrations move to a one-shot `migrate` service with `depends_on: { migrate: { condition: service_completed_successfully } }` (Compose "Control startup order").
- Bad, because emails, and group names, that differ only in accents are now two.
- Neutral, because no data is kept: the owner sets the hub up again after the move (spec §5).
- Neutral, because the `fork/main` line keeps MySQL ([ADR-0019](0019-keep-two-product-lines.md)); its copy of ADR-0004 is unchanged.

## Implementation Plan

- **Affected paths**: `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `drizzle.config.ts`, `db/index.ts`, `db/migrate.ts`, `db/schema/{enums,users,apps,groups,app-grants,directory,password-reset-tokens,settings,index}.ts`, `db/migrations/<timestamp>_baseline/`, `lib/data/{db-errors,email,users,setup,groups,apps,directory,settings}.ts`, `lib/error-log.ts`, `lib/action-failure.ts`, `lib/dify/{errors,route}.ts`, `lib/ids.ts`, `lib/auth/options.ts`, `app/api/auth/{forgot,reset}-password/route.ts`, `app/(user)/chat/[appId]/page.tsx`, `app/api/apps/[appId]/icon/route.ts`, `app/(admin)/{app,user,group}-management/{actions,schemas}.ts`, `docker-compose{,.local,.e2e,.dev}.yml`, `.env.template`, `.env.e2e`, `vitest.config.ts`, `__tests__/db/`, `e2e/global-setup.ts`, `e2e/fixtures/{db,users,access,directory,branding}.ts` and the specs with raw SQL, `docs/auth-gate.md`, `docs/ldap.md`.
- **Dependencies**: add `pg` ^8.23.1 and (dev) `@types/pg` ^8.23.1; `drizzle-orm` and `drizzle-kit` `1.0.0-rc.4`, exact; remove `mysql2`.
- **Patterns to follow**: new tables in `drizzle-orm/pg-core` with `uuid` ids, `text`, `timestamp` with time zone and no precision, `jsonb` read through a zod parse; an index on every foreign-key column that is not the leading column of a primary key; enums for static sets only, exported from `db/schema/index.ts`; email lookups through `emailMatches`; ids from outside through `idSchema`/`isId`; refusals through the `lib/data/db-errors.ts` predicates, with the constraint's name where a table has several unique indexes; upserts with `onConflictDoUpdate`, claims with `onConflictDoNothing().returning()`; write counts from `rowCount ?? 0`; a test in `__tests__/db/` for any behaviour that rests on an index, a constraint or a conflict clause.
- **Patterns to avoid**: `varchar(n)` by default; a timestamp precision; JSON in `text`; `eq(users.email, …)`; a case-insensitive match written as `LIKE` (use `ilike` or `lower()`); catching a database error inside a transaction without a savepoint; logging a database error's `detail`, message or parameters; `drizzle-kit push`; Dify's PostgreSQL; a migration that adds an enum value and uses it in the same deploy.
- **Configuration**: `.env`: `POSTGRES_DB`, `POSTGRES_USER`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` replace `MYSQL_*`, and `DATABASE_URL=postgres://<user>:<password>@postgres:5432/<db>`; `.env.development.local`: the same database on `127.0.0.1:5432`; `.env.e2e`: `postgres://e2e:e2e@127.0.0.1:5433/e2e`.
- **Migration steps** (spec §5, the owner): take the old stack down (`docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml down` on the old checkout; its project and MySQL volume are `dify-app-hub-local`'s), edit `.env` and `.env.development.local`, bring the new stack up, run first-run setup, add the apps, groups and settings again; the old MySQL volume can then be removed.

- [ ] `drizzle.config.ts` says `dialect: 'postgresql'`, and `db/migrations/` holds one folder, `<timestamp>_baseline`.
- [ ] `pnpm exec next typegen && pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, oxlint and oxfmt are clean; `pnpm test` passes.
- [ ] `pnpm test:db` passes.
- [ ] `pnpm test:ldap` passes.
- [ ] `pnpm test:e2e` passes on `desktop-light`, `desktop-dark` and `mobile-light` against the throwaway PostgreSQL, the new cases included.
- [ ] The Docker gate: the migration applied to an empty volume; `/init` signed out answers 200 on the fresh database and 307 to `/login` after setup; after `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml restart postgres` the hub answers `/api/health` 200; the `CLAUDE.md` curl checks pass.
- [ ] The owner's check on the local stack.

## Pros and Cons of the Options

### PostgreSQL 18 in the hub's own container

- Good, because the hub's upgrades, backups and restarts stay its own, and the same service runs in every compose file.
- Bad, because the production server runs a second PostgreSQL beside Dify's (memory and backups).

### The PostgreSQL Dify already runs

- Good, because one server to run and back up.
- Bad, because the hub would share Dify's upgrades, restarts and version, and a Dify upgrade could take the hub down with it.

### Stay on MySQL 8.4

- Good, because nothing changes.
- Bad, because Parts 1 to 3 would be designed around MySQL's `json` and the owner's other projects and Dify run PostgreSQL; ADR-0004's reason is gone (ADR-0022).

### `pg` (node-postgres)

- Good, because it is Drizzle's get-started driver, allows type parsers per query, sends no prepared statements by default, and is in Next's default `serverExternalPackages`.
- Bad, because its pool needs an explicit error listener and an explicit connect timeout (both set in `db/index.ts`).

### postgres.js

- Good, because it is fast and documented by Drizzle.
- Bad, because it prepares statements by default, which "you may need to opt out of" behind a pooler (DV 1a).

### A fresh start

- Good, because one baseline replaces twelve MySQL migrations and no data migration is written or tested.
- Bad, because the owner sets the hub up again (decision 14 accepts it: no data has to survive).

### Copying the data

- Good, because accounts, apps and grants would survive.
- Bad, because a one-off copy tool would be written, tested and thrown away for data the owner does not need.

### `uuid` ids

- Good, because the database enforces the format, and every id is the hub's own.
- Bad, because a malformed id raises `22P02` instead of matching nothing, so every id from outside is checked first.

### `text` ids

- Good, because a malformed id simply matches nothing.
- Bad, because the type check is given up.

### A `lower(email)` index with `lower()` on both sides

- Good, because both PostgreSQL's expression-index page and Drizzle's case-insensitive email guide document it, and the same function builds the index and runs the lookup.
- Bad, because accents now count, and every lookup must go through the helper.

### `citext`

- Good, because comparisons ignore case without a helper.
- Bad, because PostgreSQL's own page steers away from it: "Consider using nondeterministic collations … instead of this module" (DV 3b).

### A nondeterministic collation

- Good, because it can ignore case and accents together, as MySQL's collation did.
- Bad, because PostgreSQL says "their use leads to a performance penalty … they should be used only in cases where they are specifically wanted", B-tree deduplication is lost, and `ILIKE` and regular expressions do not support them (DV 3b).

### A partial unique index for the owner

- Good, because the database itself refuses a second owner, whatever the isolation level (PostgreSQL Example 11.3).
- Bad, because the loser of a race learns it from a `23505`, which `createOwner` maps to `forbidden`.

### REPEATABLE READ locking

- Good, because it is what B2 built on MySQL.
- Bad, because PostgreSQL has no gap locks, so a locking read of an empty table locks nothing, and REPEATABLE READ adds `40001` failures to retry (DV 7).

### Migrating in the entrypoint

- Good, because one container needs nothing more, as today (decision 25).
- Bad, because `migrate()` takes no lock, so several containers starting together race.

### A one-shot `migrate` service

- Good, because it migrates once before any app container starts (Compose `service_completed_successfully`).
- Bad, because it is a second service to run, not needed for one container; it is the follow-up before scaling out.
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Rewrite the owner-password recovery in `docs/auth-gate.md` for psql**

psql interpolates its variables only in what it reads: a `-c` command "must be either a command string that is completely parsable by the server (i.e., it contains no psql-specific features), or a single backslash command", and `:'name'` "quote[s] the value of a variable as an SQL literal", dealing "correctly with quotes and other special characters" (PostgreSQL 18 "psql", `-c` and "SQL Interpolation"). So the statement goes in on standard input and the hash as a psql variable; `docker compose exec` keeps standard input open by default.

On line 44, replace:

```
The password is read without echo, so it stays out of the shell history, and the hash reaches MySQL through an environment variable, so the shell never expands its `$2b$12$…`.
```

with:

```
The password is read without echo, so it stays out of the shell history, and the hash reaches PostgreSQL through an environment variable and a psql variable (`:'hash'` quotes it as an SQL literal; psql interpolates only what it reads on standard input, never a `-c` command, PostgreSQL 18 "psql"), so no shell expands its `$2b$12$…`.
```

Replace the third line of the code block (line 49):

```bash
  docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml exec -T -e HASH="$HASH" mysql sh -c 'mysql -uroot -p"$MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD" "$MYSQL_DATABASE" -e "UPDATE users SET password = '\''$HASH'\'', session_version = session_version + 1 WHERE role = '\''owner'\''"'
```

with:

```bash
  printf '%s\n' "UPDATE users SET password = :'hash', session_version = session_version + 1 WHERE role = 'owner';" | docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml exec -T -e HASH="$HASH" postgres sh -c 'psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -U "$POSTGRES_USER" -d "$POSTGRES_DB" -v hash="$HASH"'
```

On line 57, replace

```
(MySQL error 3819)
```

with

```
(SQLSTATE 23514, `check_violation`)
```

```markdown
- Emails are unique and matched without case, but with accents (ADR-0031): the unique index `users_email_key` is on `lower(email)` and every lookup goes through `emailMatches` (`lib/data/email.ts`), `lower()` on both sides. `jose@example.com` and `josé@example.com` are two accounts, where MySQL's `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` collation treated them as one.
```

The same pipe with a statement that writes nothing (`docker-compose.e2e.yml`'s `postgres`, Task 3; a bcrypt hash holds only `$`, `.`, `/`, letters and digits):

```bash
docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml up -d --wait postgres
HASH='$2b$12$abcdefghijklmnopqrstuuK1rWmZcM8m6ZVvq0yXlS6pQhE9Wb4Wa'
printf '%s\n' "SELECT :'hash' AS hash;" | docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml exec -T -e HASH="$HASH" postgres sh -c 'psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -U "$POSTGRES_USER" -d "$POSTGRES_DB" -v hash="$HASH"'
unset HASH
```

Expected: one row whose `hash` is `$2b$12$abcdefghijklmnopqrstuuK1rWmZcM8m6ZVvq0yXlS6pQhE9Wb4Wa`, character for character (no shell expanded a `$`).

### Task 14: Final verification and the Docker gate

**Files:** none changed. The session stops once in this task (Step 7) for the owner's `.env` edits, which it never reads, prints or makes itself.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: everything above; the owner's `.env` and `.env.development.local` after spec §5's edits.

- [ ] **Step 1: Static gates on the whole tree**

Run: `pnpm exec next typegen && pnpm exec tsc --noEmit && pnpm exec oxlint && pnpm exec oxfmt --check .` Expected: tsc reports nothing; oxlint shows only its existing baseline (`docs/frontend-conventions.md`); oxfmt reports nothing.

- [ ] **Step 2: The unit suite**

Run: `pnpm test` Expected: all pass. Note the file and test counts for the report and `.cii-assessment.md` #19 (Task 13).

- [ ] **Step 3: The live database suite**

Run: `pnpm test:db` Expected: all pass (`schema`, `errors`, `email`, `owner` and `directory` files); the teardown stops the e2e PostgreSQL.

- [ ] **Step 4: The directory suite**

Run: `pnpm test:ldap` Expected: all pass (spec §6: the sync's claim and memberships changed in Task 8).

- [ ] **Step 5: No MySQL driver or dialect left (spec §9)**

Run: `git grep -nE "mysql2|mysql-core|drizzle-orm/mysql|mysql:8" -- ':!docs/**' ':!README.md' ':!CHANGELOG.md' ':!.cii-assessment.md' ':!packages/**'` Expected: no output. Then `grep -c "mysql2" pnpm-lock.yaml` Expected: `0`.

- [ ] **Step 6: The e2e suite on an empty PostgreSQL**

Stop `pnpm dev` if it runs (Next 16 allows one `next dev` per checkout). Run: `docker compose -f docker-compose.e2e.yml --profile ldap down -v && pnpm test:e2e` Expected: the setup project creates the owner through `/init` (its "first-run" annotation reads "owner created through /init"), then 0 failed on `desktop-light`, `desktop-dark` and `mobile-light`, the new cases of Task 11 included.

- [ ] **Step 7: Stop and ask the owner for the `.env` edits (spec §5 steps 1–2)**

Send the owner this, and wait for their word before Step 8:

> Part 0 is ready for the Docker gate. Please edit your two git-ignored files (I do not read them):
>
> 1. In `.env`: remove `MYSQL_DATABASE`, `MYSQL_USER`, `MYSQL_PASSWORD` and `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD`; add `POSTGRES_DB`, `POSTGRES_USER` and `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (the password must not be empty, docker-library postgres); set `DATABASE_URL=postgres://<user>:<password>@postgres:5432/<db>` with the same three values. A password with characters such as `@`, `:`, `/` or `#` is written percent-encoded inside `DATABASE_URL` (it is a URL; node-postgres parses it with `pg-connection-string`), and as it is in `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`.
> 2. In `.env.development.local`: set `DATABASE_URL` to the same database on `127.0.0.1:5432` (`postgres://<user>:<password>@127.0.0.1:5432/<db>`), for `pnpm dev` on the host.
>
> Then tell me, and say whether I may take down the old MySQL stack (`dify-app-hub-local`) and start the new one. Its MySQL volume stays until you remove it after you have checked the new stack: `docker volume rm dify-app-hub-local_mysql-data`.

- [ ] **Step 8: Take down the old stack (spec §5 step 3)**

The branch's `docker-compose.local.yml` names the new project, so the old one is addressed by its project name (Compose CLI, `-p, --project-name`; `--dry-run` shows the steps without running them). Run: `docker compose -p dify-app-hub-local down --dry-run` Expected: it would stop and remove `dify-app-hub-local-app` and `dify-app-hub-local-mysql`. Then run: `docker compose -p dify-app-hub-local down` Expected: both containers removed; the volume `dify-app-hub-local_mysql-data` kept (`down` removes volumes only with `-v`).

- [ ] **Step 9: Build and start the new stack (spec §5 step 4)**

Run: `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml up -d --build` Expected: about two minutes (80 s of it `next build`; this machine has about 5 GB of RAM, and the old stack is down). If the build is killed for memory, do not restart it unprompted (CLAUDE.md). Then `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml ps` Expected: `ai-app-hub-local-postgres` running and `(healthy)`, `ai-app-hub-local-app` running.

- [ ] **Step 10: The fresh volume's first start (Review Focus 2)**

Run each:

- `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml logs app | grep -c "Database migration complete"` Expected: `1`.
- `docker inspect -f '{{.RestartCount}}' ai-app-hub-local-app` Expected: `0` (the migration did not meet a server that was not yet listening on TCP).
- `docker inspect -f '{{range .Mounts}}{{.Destination}} {{end}}' ai-app-hub-local-postgres` Expected: `/var/lib/postgresql` (DV 7b).
- `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml exec -T postgres sh -c 'psql -U "$POSTGRES_USER" -d "$POSTGRES_DB" -tAc "SELECT name FROM drizzle.__drizzle_migrations"'` Expected: one line, `<14 digits>_baseline`.

- [ ] **Step 11: The signed-out checks (CLAUDE.md's list, on the fresh database)**

Run each with `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code} %{redirect_url}\n' <url>` unless a body is named:

- `localhost:5300/api/health` → `200`.
- `localhost:5300/init` → `200` (spec §6: first-run setup is open on the fresh database).
- `localhost:5300/apps` → `307 http://localhost:5300/login?callbackUrl=%2Fapps`.
- `curl -s localhost:5300/api/dify/3b241101-e2bb-4255-8caf-4136c566a962/parameters` → `401` with `{"code":"unauthorized",…}`; the same 401 envelope for `/api/client/apps`, `/api/users` and `/api/init/status` (the proxy denies unknown `/api/*` paths before routing).
- `curl -s localhost:5300/api/branding/logo` → `404` with `{"code":"not_found",…}` while `BRANDING_MODE` is unset in `.env` (a `database`-mode `.env` with no saved logo also answers 404; a 401 would mean the proxy's public list lost the route).
- `curl -s localhost:5300/login | grep -c 'href="/forgot-password"'` → `0` while SMTP is off.
- `curl -s localhost:5300/login | grep -c 'id="antd-cssinjs"'` → `1`.

- [ ] **Step 12: First-run setup, then the closed `/init`**

Ask the owner to open `http://localhost:5300/init` and create the owner (the database is new, decision 14). After their word: `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code} %{redirect_url}\n' localhost:5300/init` Expected: `307 http://localhost:5300/login`.

- [ ] **Step 13: A PostgreSQL restart under the running hub (Review Focus 3)**

The pool closes a client idle for 10 seconds (node-postgres "Pool", `idleTimeoutMillis` default), so the restart follows a request at once, while that client is still idle. Run: `curl -s -o /dev/null localhost:5300/api/health && docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml restart postgres` Then: `curl --fail --retry 15 --retry-delay 2 --retry-all-errors -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' localhost:5300/api/health` Expected: `200` once PostgreSQL is back (curl `--retry-all-errors` retries the 500s while it is down). Then:

- `docker inspect -f '{{.RestartCount}}' ai-app-hub-local-app` Expected: still `0` (the process did not crash).
- `docker compose -f docker-compose.local.yml logs app | grep "Database pool"` Expected: at least one line `Database pool: { name: 'error', code: '57P01' }`, with no message, no detail and no stack.

- [ ] **Step 14: Report to the owner**

Report every result above, then the checks left for the browser:

- sign in as the owner, then once more with the email typed in capitals;
- add an app (the owner's Dify), chat with it, delete it;
- create a group and a user-role account, restrict an app to the group, sign in as that account;
- if `LDAP_*` is set: a directory sign-in and "Sync now" on the users page;
- set the brand on Settings > Branding if `BRANDING_MODE=database`.

Then remind them of the steps after the merge: the GitHub repo and the local folders are renamed between sessions, from the spec's §8 (`CLAUDE.md` points there, Task 13), and the old MySQL volume can go once the new stack is checked. ADR-0031 stays `proposed` until the owner's check; the PR is opened only on their word.
